Gradient higher integrability for degenerate/ singular parabolic multi-phase problems
Abhrojyoti Sen

Abstract
. This article establishes an interior gradient higher integrability result for weak solutions to parabolic multi-phase problems. The prototype equation for the parabolic multi-phase problem of p p -Laplace type is given by u t − div ( | ∇ u | p − 2 ∇ u + a ( z ) | ∇ u | q − 2 ∇ u + b ( z ) | ∇ u | s − 2 ∇ u ) = 0 , u_{t}-\operatorname{div}\left(|\nabla u|^{p-2}\nabla u+a(z)|\nabla u|^{q-2}\nabla u+b(z)|\nabla u|^{s-2}\nabla u\right)=0, where 2 ​ n n + 2 < p ≤ q ≤ s < ∞ \frac{2n}{n+2}<p\leq q\leq s<\infty , and the coefficients a ⁡ ( z ) a(z) and b ⁡ ( z ) b(z) are non-negative Hölder continuous functions on Ω T = Ω × ( 0 , T ) \Omega_{T}=\Omega\times(0,T) , with Ω ⊂ ℝ n \Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n} . We introduce a novel intrinsic scaling to address the problem in both the degenerate regime ( p ≥ 2 p\geq 2 ) and the singular regime ( 2 ​ n n + 2 < p < 2 ) , \left(\frac{2n}{n+2}<p<2\right), providing a unified framework. Our approach involves proving uniform parabolic Sobolev-Poincaré inequalities, which are key to establishing reverse Hölder type inequalities, along with covering lemmas for the p p , ( p , q ) (p,q) , ( p , s ) (p,s) , and ( p , q , s ) (p,q,s) -phases without distinguishing between the regimes of p p , q q , and s s . In the end, we also discuss the gradient higher integrability for general parabolic multi-phase problem involving a finite number of phases.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary: 35B65, 35D30, 35K55, 35K65, 35K67; Secondary: 35F20.

1.
Introduction and main result

1.1.
Overview

In this paper, we are interested in studying the gradient higher integrability for weak solutions to

(1.1)

\displaystyle u_{t}-\dv{\mathcal{A}}\left(z,\nabla u\right)=0\,\,\,\,\text{in}\,\,\,\,\Omega_{T},

where
z=(x,t)\in\Omega_{T}:=\Omega\times(0,T)
denotes a space-time cylinder, with
\Omega\subset\mathds{R}^{n}
being a bounded domain for
n\geq 2
and
{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies the conditions outlined in
Assumption

1.1
(see
Section

1.2
). Here, the function
u:\Omega_{T}\to\mathds{R}^{N}
with
N\geq 1,
where
\nabla u
represents the spatial gradient of
u
and
u_{t}
denotes the time derivative. The local higher integrability of the gradient for weak solutions
u
to the elliptic
p
-Laplace equation is a well-established result; it shows that if the weak solution
u
is in
W^{1,p}_{loc}(\Omega),
then
u
belongs to
W^{1,p+\varepsilon}_{loc}(\Omega)
for some
\varepsilon>0
(see
[
29
]
). This phenomenon was first observed by Gehring
[
19
]
in the context of the Jacobian of a quasi-conformal mapping. The proof for elliptic equations and systems is based on the energy estimate- specifically, an estimate that has the structure of a reverse Poincaré inequality- and a Sobolev-Poincaré inequality that yields certain reverse Hölder type inequalities for
\nabla u.
The final proof of higher integrability for
\nabla u
follows from an application of Gehring’s type lemma.
As it is well known by now, although the proof for the higher integrability of
\nabla u
where
u
is a weak solution to the parabolic
p
-Laplace equation broadly follows a similar strategy as in the elliptic case, there are certain genuine difficulties. One of the main difficulties is when
p\neq 2,
the parabolic system exhibits a nonhomogeneous scaling, that is, a constant multiple (except
0
and
1
) of a solution to the parabolic system no longer remains a solution. However, a scale-invariant reverse Hölder type inequality is crucial for establishing higher integrability. To overcome this difficulty, DiBenedetto-Friedman
[
13
,
16
,
17
,
18
]
introduced scaled parabolic cylinders, known as intrinsic cylinders, whose scaling parameter depends on the local behaviour of the solution. The subsequent step in the proof involves demonstrating the existence of these intrinsic cylinders on a suitable superlevel set using a stopping time argument. The final step in establishing higher integrability combines these results with reverse Hölder inequalities derived for the intrinsic cylinders. The first result establishing the gradient higher integrability of weak solutions to the parabolic
p
-Laplace equation in the range
\frac{2n}{n+2}<p<\infty
is due to Kinnunen-Lewis
[
26
]
. Also, see
[
27
]
for the gradient higher integrability of the very weak solutions.

In the following paragraphs, we will review some existing regularity results for elliptic and parabolic double-phase and multi-phase problems. The elliptic double-phase system reads as:

(1.2)

\displaystyle-\dv\left(|\nabla u|^{p-2}\nabla u+a(x)|\nabla u|^{q-2}\nabla u\right)=0\,\,\,\,\text{in}\,\,\,\,\Omega,

where
a(x)\in C^{\alpha}(\Omega)
for some
\alpha\in(0,1].
Esposito et. al.
[
14
]
were the first to establish the gradient higher integrability of weak solutions to (
1.2
) under the conditions:

\displaystyle 1<p<q<p+\frac{\alpha p}{n}

with an additional assumption that
\nabla u\in L^{q}(\Omega).
The related regularity results, including Harnack’s inequality, Hölder estimates and Calderón-Zygmund type estimates can be found in
[
3
,
8
,
11
,
7
]
. Moreover, Colombo-Mingione
[
9
]
established the gradient Hölder regularity under a sharp condition on
p
and
q,
i.e.,

\displaystyle q\leq p+\alpha

which is independent of
n.

From a more technical point of view, a typical approach to addressing double phase problems is to first consider a fixed ball
B_{R}
along with the condition

(1.3)

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in B_{R}}\frac{a(x)}{R^{\alpha}}\leq M

for some threshold quantity
M.
If the condition (
1.3
) is satisfied, it indicates that the coefficient
a(x)
remains sufficiently small in
B_{R},
placing us in the
p
-phase. Conversely, if (
1.3
) does not hold, specifically if,

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in B_{R}}\frac{a(x)}{R^{\alpha}}>M,

then the influence of
q
-growth in (
1.2
) dominates and we are in the
(p,q)
-phase.

The elliptic multi-phase equation reads as follows:

\displaystyle-\operatorname*{div}\left(|Du|^{p-2}Du+\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}(x)|Du|^{p_{i}-2}Du\right)=-\operatorname*{div}\left(|F|^{p-2}F+\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}(x)|F|^{p_{i}-2}F\right)

in
\Omega
, where
1<p<p_{1}\leq\cdots\leq p_{m}
and
0\leq a_{i}(\cdot)\in C^{0,\alpha_{i}}(\overline{\Omega})
with
\alpha_{i}\in(0,1]
for all
i\in\{1,\cdots,m\}
.
Similarly to the assumptions made in the double-phase case, we impose the following condition:

\frac{p_{i}}{p}\leq 1+\frac{\alpha_{i}}{n}\qquad\text{for all }i\in\{1,\cdots,m\}.

De Filippis-Oh
[
12
]
established that the gradient of a local minimizer of the multi-phase variational problem is Hölder continuous and satisfies intrinsic Morrey decay. Additionally, De Filippis
[
10
]
proved Calderón-Zygmund type estimates, while Baasandorj-Byun-Oh
[
2
]
investigated gradient estimates in the Orlicz-type multi-phase equations. Moreover, Fang-Rădulescu-Zhang-Zhang
[
15
]
provided gradient estimates within the framework of Campanato spaces.

Although a substantial body of literature exists on the regularity theory for elliptic double-phase problems, the investigation of parabolic double-phase problems remains relatively sparse. Recent work by Chlebicka et al.
[
6
]
and Singer
[
30
]
has established the existence of weak solutions for parabolic double-phase problems. The first regularity result concerning the parabolic double phase problem is the local gradient higher integrability of weak solutions to the following equation:

\displaystyle u_{t}-\operatorname*{div}\left(|Du|^{p-2}Du+a(z)|Du|^{q-2}Du\right)=-\operatorname*{div}\left(|F|^{p-2}F+a(z)|F|^{q-2}F\right)

due to Kim-Kinnunen-Moring
[
24
]
. Their result is established for the case
2\leq p\leq q<\infty.
On the other hand, Kim-Särkiö
[
25
]
addressed the case where
\frac{2n}{n+2}<p<2.
It is worth mentioning that Hästö-Ok
[
20
]
studied the gradient higher integrability for parabolic
(p,q)
-growth problems with constant coefficients, specifically when
a(z)=a_{0}>0.
In their work, Kim-Kinnunen-Moring
[
24
]
introduced appropriate alternatives and two types of intrinsic cylinders. For the
p
-phase, they defined
p
-intrinsic cylinders as:

\displaystyle Q^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0})=B_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times(t_{0}-\lambda^{2-p}\varrho^{2},t_{0}+\lambda^{2-p}\varrho^{2})

and for
(p,q)
-phase, they considered the
(p,q)
-intrinsic cylinders given by:

\displaystyle G^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0})=B_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times\left(t_{0}-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{p}+a(z_{0})\lambda^{q}}\varrho^{2},t_{0}+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{p}+a(z_{0})\lambda^{q}}\varrho^{2}\right)

are considered for the
(p,q)
-phase. On the other hand, Kim-Särkiö
[
25
]
introduced different intrinsic cylinders for
p
-phase and
(p,q)
-phase:

\displaystyle Q^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0})=B_{\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho}(x_{0})\times(t_{0}-\varrho^{2},t_{0}+\varrho^{2})

and

\displaystyle G^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0})=B_{\lambda^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\varrho}(x_{0})\times\left(t_{0}-\frac{\lambda^{p}}{\lambda^{p}+a(z_{0})\lambda^{q}}\varrho^{2},t_{0}+\frac{\lambda^{p}}{\lambda^{p}+a(z_{0})\lambda^{q}}\varrho^{2}\right),

respectively. A notable characteristic of these intrinsic cylinders is that, for the case
p\geq 2
, both types of intrinsic cylinder shrink in the time scale as
\lambda\to\infty.
In contrast, for the case
p<2,
the
p
-intrinsic cylinders shrink in the spatial scale as
\lambda\to\infty.
Furthermore, in this latter scenario, since
p\leq q,
the
(p,q)
-intrinsic cylinders shrink in both time and space scales as
\lambda
approaches infinity.

The primary objective of this paper is to establish a gradient higher integrability result for bounded weak solutions to (
1.1
), which is modeled after the following equation:

\displaystyle u_{t}-\operatorname{div}\left(|\nabla u|^{p-2}\nabla u+a(z)|\nabla u|^{q-2}\nabla u+b(z)|\nabla u|^{s-2}\nabla u\right)=0,\,\,\,\text{in}\,\,\,\Omega_{T}.

In a recent paper by Kim-Oh
[
21
]
, the authors established gradient higher integrability for the case
2\leq p\leq q<\infty
(the degenerate case). In this work, we focus on the singular regime, specifically for
\frac{2n}{n+2}<p\leq 2.
We further assume that the non-negative coefficients
a(\cdot),b(\cdot)
are
\alpha
- and
\beta
-Hölder continuous, respectively, for some
\alpha,\beta\in(0,1]
under conditions
q\leq p+\frac{2\alpha}{n+2}
and
s\leq p+\frac{2\beta}{n+2}.
In
[
21
]
, four types of intrinsic cylinders and alternatives were introduced to achieve the goal of gradient higher integrability. In this study, we propose the use of
\mu
-modified intrinsic cylinders (see
(v)
,
Section

1.2.1
) to facilitate our analysis. From the definition of
\mu
provided in
(iv)
,
Section

1.2.1
, specifically,

\frac{n}{n+2}<\mu\leq\min\left\{1,\frac{p}{2}\right\},

it is evident that the cylinders shrink in both time and spatial scales simultaneously as
\lambda\to\infty.
The introduction of these cylinders offers the significant advantage of enabling a unified treatment of both the singular case
\left(\frac{2n}{n+2}<p<2\right)
and the degenerate (
p\geq 2
) case. The motivation for utilizing such intrinsic cylinders is inspired by the work of Adimurthi-Byun-Oh
[
1
]
, which presents a unified approach to achieving boundary gradient higher integrability for very weak solutions to parabolic systems with
p(z)
-growth
[
4
]
. Notably, similar results were previously established by Bögelein-Li
[
5
]
for the case
p(z)\geq 2
and by Li
[
28
]
for the case
\frac{2n}{n+2}<p(z)\leq 2,
but handled separately.

1.2.
Definitions and Notations

In this section, we introduce some basic definitions and notations. We start with the definition of the parabolic metric.

Definition 1.1

(Parabolic metric)
.

Given any two points
z_{1}=(x_{1},t_{1})
and
z_{2}=(x_{2},t_{2})\in\mathds{R}^{n+1},
we define the parabolic metric
d
as

\displaystyle d(z_{1},z_{2}):=\max\left\{|x_{1}-x_{2}|,\sqrt{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}\right\}.

A suitable adaptation of the above definition in the intrinsically scaled cylinders centered at
z_{0}\in\mathds{R}^{n+1}
is as follows.

Definition 1.2

(Scaled parabolic metric)
.

Fix
z_{0}\in\mathds{R}^{n+1}.
Given any two points
z_{1}=(x_{1},t_{1})
and
z_{2}=(x_{2},t_{2})\in\mathds{R}^{n+1}
with
K\geq 1,\lambda\geq 1,\mu>0,
we define the parabolic metric
d^{\lambda,\mu}_{z_{0}}
as

\displaystyle\displaystyle d^{\lambda,\mu}_{z_{0}}(z_{1},z_{2})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\max\left\{\lambda^{1-\mu}|x_{1}-x_{2}|,\sqrt{\lambda^{p-2\mu}|t_{1}-t_{2}|}\right\},\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if}\,\,\,K\lambda^{p}\geq a(z_{0})\lambda^{q}\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,K\lambda^{p}\geq b(z_{0})\lambda^{s},\\
\max\left\{\lambda^{1-\mu}|x_{1}-x_{2}|,\sqrt{(\lambda^{p}+a(z_{0})\lambda^{q})\lambda^{-2\mu}|t_{1}-t_{2}|}\right\},\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if}\,\,\,K\lambda^{p}<a(z_{0})\lambda^{q}\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,K\lambda^{p}\geq b(z_{0})\lambda^{s},\\
\max\left\{\lambda^{1-\mu}|x_{1}-x_{2}|,\sqrt{(\lambda^{p}+b(z_{0})\lambda^{s})\lambda^{-2\mu}|t_{1}-t_{2}|}\right\},\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if}\,\,\,K\lambda^{p}\geq a(z_{0})\lambda^{q}\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,K\lambda^{p}<b(z_{0})\lambda^{s},\\
\max\left\{\lambda^{1-\mu}|x_{1}-x_{2}|,\sqrt{\Lambda\lambda^{-2\mu}|t_{1}-t_{2}|}\right\},\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if}\,\,\,K\lambda^{p}<a(z_{0})\lambda^{q}\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,K\lambda^{p}<b(z_{0})\lambda^{s},\end{array}\right.

where
\Lambda:=\lambda^{p}+a(z_{0})\lambda^{q}+b(z_{0})\lambda^{s}.

We fix the following radii.

Definition 1.3
.

For any
\varrho>0,
we fix two radii
\rho_{a}
and
\rho_{b}
such that

\displaystyle 0<\varrho\leq\rho_{a}<\rho_{b}\leq 4\varrho\,\,\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,\,2\varrho\leq\rho_{b}\leq 4\varrho\,\,\,\text{with}\,\,\,0<h_{0}<\frac{\rho_{b}^{2}-\rho_{a}^{2}}{4}

be very small number.

We set the following structural assumptions on
{\mathcal{A}}
in (
1.1
).

Assumption 1.1
.

We assume
{\mathcal{A}}(z,\xi):\Omega_{T}\times\mathds{R}^{Nn}\to\mathds{R}^{Nn}
with
N\geq 1
is a Carathéodory vector field satisfying the following structural assumptions:

A1.

\langle{\mathcal{A}}(z,\xi),\xi\rangle\geq\mathcal{C}_{0}\left(|\xi|^{p}+a(z)|\xi|^{q}+b(z)|\xi|^{s}\right)
and
|{\mathcal{A}}(z,\xi)|\leq\mathcal{C}_{1}\left(|\xi|^{p-1}+a(z)|\xi|^{q-1}+b(z)|\xi|^{s-1}\right)
holds for every
z\in\Omega_{T}
and
\xi\in\mathds{R}^{Nn}
. Here,
\mathcal{C}_{0},\mathcal{C}_{1}
are positive constants with
\mathcal{C}_{0}\leq\mathcal{C}_{1}<\infty.

A2.

The coefficients
a:\Omega_{T}\to\mathds{R}^{+}
is in
C^{\alpha,\frac{\alpha}{2}}(\Omega_{T})
for some
\alpha\in(0,1]
and
b:\Omega_{T}\to\mathds{R}^{+}
is in
C^{\beta,\frac{\beta}{2}}(\Omega_{T})
for some
\beta\in(0,1]
and we impose the restriction

(1.4)

\displaystyle\frac{2n}{n+2}<p\leq q\leq p+\min\left\{\alpha\left(\frac{p}{2}-\frac{n}{n+2}\right),\frac{2\alpha}{n+2}\right\}<\infty,

\displaystyle\frac{2n}{n+2}<p\leq s\leq p+\min\left\{\beta\left(\frac{p}{2}-\frac{n}{n+2}\right),\frac{2\beta}{n+2}\right\}<\infty.

In particular, this means that
a,b\in L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})
and

\displaystyle|a(x_{1},t_{1})-a(x_{2},t_{2})|\leq[a]_{\alpha}\left(|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\alpha}+|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right),

\displaystyle|b(x_{1},t_{1})-b(x_{2},t_{2})|\leq[b]_{\beta}\left(|x_{2}-x_{2}|^{\beta}+|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\right),

for every
(x_{1},t_{1}),(x_{2},t_{2})\in\Omega_{T}.

Let us first recall the definition of weak solution to
Eq.

1.1
:

Definition 1.4
.

A function
u:\Omega\times(0,T)\to\mathds{R}^{N}
with

\displaystyle u\in C_{loc}\left(0,T;L^{2}_{loc}\left(\Omega,\mathds{R}^{N}\right)\right)\cap L^{s}_{loc}\left(0,T;W^{1,s}_{loc}\left(\Omega,\mathds{R}^{N}\right)\right)

is said to be a weak solution to (
1.1
) if for every compact subset
K\subset\Omega
and for every subinterval
[t_{1},t_{2}]\subset(0,T]

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{K}-u\cdot\varphi_{t}\,dx\,dt+\int_{K}u\varphi dx\Big|_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{K}{\mathcal{A}}(z,\nabla u)\cdot\nabla\varphi\,dx\,dt=0

holds for all nonnegative test function
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(K\times(t_{1},t_{2}),\mathds{R}^{N}).

1.2.1.
Notations

Here we collect the standard notation that will be used throughout the paper:

(i)

We shall denote
n
to be the space dimension and by
z=(x,t)
to be a point in
\mathds{R}^{n}\times(0,T)
. Moreover, we will always assume
n\geq 2
.

(ii)

A ball with center
x_{0}\in{\mathds{R}^{n}}
and radius
r
is denoted as

\displaystyle B_{r}(x_{0})=\left\{x\in{\mathds{R}^{n}}|\,\,\,|x-x_{0}|<r\right\}.

(iii)

In general, a parabolic cylinder centered at
z_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0})
is denoted as

\displaystyle Q_{r,\varrho}(z_{0})=B_{r}(x_{0})\times\left(t_{0}-\varrho,t_{0}+\varrho\right):=B_{r}(x_{0})\times l_{\varrho}(t_{0}).

(iv)

We shall fix a universal constant
\frac{n}{n+2}<\mu\leq\min\left\{1,\frac{p}{2}\right\}
which is possible since we always assume
p>\frac{2n}{n+2}
holds.

(v)

We use four types of cylinders for
p,

(p,q),

(p,s)
and
(p,q,s)
-phases. For
\lambda\geq 1,
the notation
{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0})
is used to denote
p
-intrinsic cylinders at
z_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0}),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0}):=B_{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\varrho}(x_{0})\times\left(t_{0}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}\varrho^{2}}{\lambda^{p}},t_{0}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}\varrho^{2}}{\lambda^{p}}\right)=:B^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times I^{\lambda,p}_{\varrho}(t_{0}).

{\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0})
is used to denote
(p,q)
-intrinsic cylinders at
z_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0}),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0}):=B_{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\varrho}(x_{0})\times\left(t_{0}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}\varrho^{2}}{\lambda^{p}+a(z_{0})\lambda^{q}},t_{0}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}\varrho^{2}}{\lambda^{p}+a(z_{0})\lambda^{q}}\right)=:B^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times I^{\lambda,(p,q)}_{\varrho}(t_{0}).

\mathcal{S}^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0})
is used to denote
(p,s)
-intrinsic cylinders at
z_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0}):=B_{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\varrho}(x_{0})\times\left(t_{0}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}\varrho^{2}}{\lambda^{p}+b(z_{0})\lambda^{s}},t_{0}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}\varrho^{2}}{\lambda^{p}+b(z_{0})\lambda^{s}}\right)=:B^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times I^{\lambda,(p,s)}_{\varrho}(t_{0}),

and
\mathcal{G}^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0})
is used to denote
(p,q,s)
-intrinsic cylinders at
z_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0})

\displaystyle:=B_{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\varrho}(x_{0})\times\left(t_{0}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}\varrho^{2}}{\lambda^{p}+a(z_{0})\lambda^{q}+b(z_{0})\lambda^{s}},t_{0}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}\varrho^{2}}{\lambda^{p}+a(z_{0})\lambda^{q}+b(z_{0})\lambda^{s}}\right)

\displaystyle=:B^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times I^{\lambda,(p,q,s)}_{\varrho}(t_{0}).

(vi)

In the case
\lambda=1
, we denote

{\mathcal{P}}_{\varrho}(z_{0}):=B_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times(t_{0}-\varrho^{2},t_{0}+\varrho^{2})=:B_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times I_{\varrho}(t_{0}).

(vii)

We shall use the notation
H(z,|\xi|):=|\xi|^{p}+a(z)|\xi|^{q}+b(z)|\xi|^{s}
for any
\xi\in{\mathds{R}^{n}}.

(viii)

Integration with respect to either space or time only will be denoted by a single integral
\int
whereas integration on
\Omega\times(0,T)
will be denoted by a double integral
\iint
.

(ix)

The notation
a\lesssim b
is shorthand for
a\leq cb
where
c
is a universal constant which depends on “
data
"
where

\displaystyle\text{{data}}:=\left(n,N,p,q,s,\mathcal{C}_{0},\mathcal{C}_{1},\alpha,\beta,[a]_{\alpha},[b]_{\beta},||a||_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})},||b||_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})},||H(z,|\nabla u|)||_{L^{1}(\Omega_{T})}\right).

(x)

We shall denote

\displaystyle(f)_{A}:=\frac{1}{|A|}\iint_{A}fdz=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{A}fdz

as the integral average of
f
over a measurable set
A\subset\Omega_{T}
with
0<|A|<\infty.

1.3.
Main result

Now we state our main result.

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
u
be a weak solution of (
1.1
) with
Assumption

1.1
in force and
\mu
be the constant defined in
Section

1.2.1

(iv)
. Then there exist positive constants
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(\textnormal{{data}})\in(0,1)
and
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})\geq 1
such that the following holds:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{r}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{1+\varepsilon}\,dz\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{2r}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz+1\right)^{1+\frac{\varepsilon s}{(n+2)\mu-n}},

for every
{\mathcal{P}}_{2r}(z_{0})\subset\Omega\times(0,T)
and any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
where
{\mathcal{P}}_{(\cdot)}(z_{0})
is defined in
Section

1.2.1

(vi)
.

Remark 1.1
.

Note that in
Definition

1.4
, we assume that
|\nabla u|\in L^{s}(\Omega_{T}).
However, a more natural assumption would be to consider
|\nabla u|\in L^{p}(\Omega_{T})
alongside the condition

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{T}}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz=\iint_{\Omega_{T}}\left(|\nabla u|^{p}+a(z)|\nabla u|^{q}+b(z)|\nabla u|^{s}\right)\,dz<\infty.

In an upcoming work
[
22
]
, we will employ a parabolic Lipschitz truncation method for the system (
1.1
) under the assumption
2\leq p\leq q<\infty.
The Lipschitz truncation method for the singular case, including in the double-phase problem, remains an open question and could be explored using this new intrinsic scaling in future work.

Remark 1.2
.

It is important to note that
Eq.

1.1
is homogeneous. The techniques developed in this work can also be applied to equations with a non-homogeneous term in divergence form. However, to simplify the exposition, we restrict our focus to the homogeneous equation.

1.3.1.
Some comments on the proof

Before proceeding, we provide an outline of the proof of the main theorem. As mentioned earlier, the strategy to establish the higher integrability of gradients for parabolic equations typically involves two main steps: first, we prove reverse Hölder-type inequalities on intrinsic cylinders, and in the second step, we show the existence of such intrinsic cylinders in certain superlevel sets. The final proof can be completed by using a Vitali covering argument alongside Fubini’s theorem. In the following, we describe each of these steps in more detail within our context.

(i)

To analyze the multi-phase phenomenon described by the equation, we consider four types of intrinsic cylinders and appropriate alternatives, namely
p,(p,q),(p,s),

(p,q,s)
-phases, as introduced in
[
21
]
. We establish reverse Hölder inequalities for each type of intrinsic cylinder. A fundamental component in deriving these reverse Hölder inequalities is the parabolic Sobolev-Poincaré-type inequality, which we first need to establish for each phase. Our parabolic Sobolev-Poincaré inequalities hold for the range
\frac{2n}{n+2}<p\leq q\leq s<\infty.
The primary tools for proving these inequalities include basic applications of Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality. Interestingly, our findings suggest that the parabolic Sobolev-Poincaré inequalities for the singular case can be viewed as a perturbation by
``\lambda"
of the inequalities for the degenerate case (see
Lemma

3.2
and
Lemma

3.8
). This perspective gives a streamlined approach to several estimates, including those in the double-phase case. Furthermore, we introduce a parameter
\mu
to modify our intrinsic cylinders, allowing for a unified handling of the problem. In particular, when
\mu=1,
we recover the intrinsic cylinders defined in
[
21
]
, and for
\mu=\frac{p}{2},
we obtain similar intrinsic cylinders as those defined in
[
25
]
for the double-phase problem.

(ii)

The next objective is to establish the existence of such intrinsic cylinders in a superlavel set and devise a precise covering argument that is applicable for all
\frac{2n}{n+2}<p\leq q\leq s<\infty.
We derive a Vitali constant
\kappa
that depends on the parameter
\mu.
Unlike
[
24
,
25
]
, our Vitali covering argument, requires addressing sixteen distinct cases. This is required to encompass all possible configurations of intrinsic cylinders, as outlined in
[
21
]
. To ensure that the exposition is self-contained and precise, we provide detailed and rigorous proofs throughout the discussion.

1.4.
Plan of the paper

The article is structured as follows: In
Section

2
, we record some known results.
Section

3
is dedicated to proving the reverse Hölder inequalities for the
p,(p,q),(p,s),(p,q,s)
-phases. In
Section

4
, we establish our gradient higher integrability result, which is stated in
Theorem

1.1
. In
Section

5
, we outline a blueprint for achieving the analogue of
Theorem

1.1
for (
5.1
).
Lastly, in
Appendix

A
, we provide a sketch of the proofs for
Lemma

3.14
and
Lemma

3.20
.

2.
Auxiliary results

In this section, we collect some auxiliary lemmas which will be used throughout the paper.
The first result is the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality (see
[
24
, Lemma 4.1]
or
[
13
, Chapter I]
).

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
\varrho>0
and
B_{\varrho}(x_{0})\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a ball. For constants
\sigma,\xi,r\in[1,\infty)
and
\vartheta\in(0,1)
satisfying
-\frac{n}{\sigma}\leq\vartheta(1-\frac{n}{\xi})-(1-\vartheta)\frac{n}{r}
and for any
h\in W^{1,\xi}(B_{\varrho}(x_{0}))
, there holds

\fint_{B_{\varrho}(x_{0})}\frac{\left|h\right|^{\sigma}}{\varrho^{\sigma}}\ dx\lesssim_{(n,\xi)}\left(\fint_{B_{\varrho}(x_{0})}\frac{\left|h\right|^{\xi}}{\varrho^{\xi}}+|\nabla h|^{\xi}\ dx\right)^{\frac{\vartheta\sigma}{\xi}}\left(\fint_{B_{\varrho}(x_{0})}\frac{\left|h\right|^{r}}{\varrho^{r}}\ dx\right)^{\frac{(1-\vartheta)\sigma}{r}}.

Next, we record the standard iteration lemma (see
[
24
, Lemma 4.2]
):

Lemma 2.2
.

Let
0<r<R<\infty
be given and
h:[r,R]\to\mathds{R}
be a non-negative and bounded function. Furthermore, let
\theta\in(0,1)
and
A,B,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\geq 0
be fixed constants and
suppose that

h(\varrho_{1})\leq\theta h(\varrho_{2})+\frac{A}{(\varrho_{2}-\varrho_{1})^{\gamma_{1}}}+\frac{B}{(\varrho_{2}-\varrho_{1})^{\gamma_{2}}},

holds for all
r\leq\varrho_{1}<\varrho_{2}\leq R
, then the following conclusion holds:

h(r)\lesssim_{(\theta,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})}\frac{A}{(R-r)^{\gamma_{1}}}+\frac{B}{(R-r)^{\gamma_{2}}}.

Below, we recall the energy estimate derived in
[
21
, Lemma 3.1]
. Note that, Kim-Oh derived the energy estimate for
2\leq p\leq q<\infty.
By using a Lipschitz truncation method as in
[
23
]
the same energy estimate can be proved for the singular case,
\frac{2n}{n+2}<p\leq 2
(see
Remark

1.1
).

To state the energy estimate, let us define appropriate cutoff functions. Assume
R_{2},S_{2}>0
with
R_{1}\in[\frac{R_{2}}{2},R_{2})
and
S_{1}\in[\frac{S_{2}}{2^{2}},S_{2}).
Furthermore, let
0<h_{0}<\frac{S_{2}-S_{1}}{4}
and
\eta\in C^{\infty}_{c}(B_{R_{2}}(x_{0}))
and
\zeta\in C^{\infty}_{c}(l_{S_{2}-h_{0}}(t_{0})).
Moreover,
\eta
and
\zeta
satisfy the following:

\displaystyle(i)\,\,\,\,\ 0\leq\eta\leq 1,\,\,\,\,\eta\equiv 1\,\,\,\text{in}\,\,\,B_{R_{1}}(x_{0}),\,\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,\,||\nabla\eta||_{\infty}\leq\frac{c}{R_{2}-R_{1}},

\displaystyle(ii)\,\,\,\,\ 0\leq\zeta\leq 1,\,\,\,\,\zeta\equiv 1\,\,\,\text{in}\,\,\,l_{S_{1}}(t_{0}),\,\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,\,||\partial_{t}\zeta||_{\infty}\leq\frac{c}{S_{2}-S_{1}}.

Then the energy estimate reads as follows.

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
). Then there exists a constant
c=c(n,p,q,s,\mathcal{C}_{0},\mathcal{C}_{1})
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in l_{S_{2}}(t_{0})}\fint_{B_{R_{2}}(x_{0})}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{Q_{R_{2},S_{2}}}\right|^{2}}{S_{2}}\eta^{s}(x)\zeta^{2}(t)dx+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{R_{2},S_{2}}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\eta^{s}(x)\zeta^{2}(t)\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{R_{2},S_{2}}(z_{0})}H\left(z,\left|\frac{u-(u)_{Q_{R_{2},S_{2}}}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\right|\right)\zeta^{2}(t)\,dx\,dt+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{R_{2},S_{2}}(z_{0})}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{Q_{R_{2},S_{2}}}\right|^{2}}{S_{2}-S_{1}}\eta^{s}(x)\zeta(t)\,dx\,dt

holds for every
Q_{R_{2},S_{2}}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}.

The next result is the parabolic Poincaré inequality which is derived in
[
21
, Lemma 3.3]
.

Lemma 2.4
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
). Then there exists a constant
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})
such that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r,\varrho}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{u-(u)_{Q_{r,\varrho}(z_{0})}}{r}\right|^{\theta m}\,dz\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r,\varrho}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta m}dz+c\left(\frac{\varrho}{r^{2}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r,\varrho}(z_{0})}\tilde{H}(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz\right)^{\theta m}

for any
Q_{r,\varrho}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}
defined in
Section

1.2.1

(iii)
with
m\in(1,s]
and
\theta\in(\frac{1}{m},1],
where

\tilde{H}(z,|\nabla u|):=|\nabla u|^{p-1}+a(z)|\nabla u|^{q-1}+b(z)|\nabla u|^{s-1}.

3.
Reverse Hölder inequalities

In this section, we will establish the reverse Hölder inequalities for the
p,(p,q),(p,s),(p,q,s)
-phases.
From this point onward, we will set

(3.1)

\displaystyle K:=2+\frac{(2[a]_{\alpha})^{\frac{n+2}{\alpha}}}{|B_{1}|}\iint_{\Omega_{T}}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz+\frac{(2[b]_{\beta})^{\frac{n+2}{\beta}}}{|B_{1}|}\iint_{\Omega_{T}}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz.

3.1.
Reverse Hölder inequality for
p
-phase

We start with defining the
p
-intrinsic cylinders.

Assumption 3.1
.

Recalling
Definition

1.3
, in the
p
-phase, we assume that the following is satisfied:

p1.

K\lambda^{p}\geq a(z_{0})\lambda^{q},\,\,\,K\lambda^{p}\geq b(z_{0})\lambda^{s}.

p2.

\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho}(z_{0})}(|\nabla u|^{p}+a(z)|\nabla u|^{q}+b(z)|\nabla u|^{s})dz<\lambda^{p}\,\,\,\text{holds for all}\,\,\,\rho\in(\rho_{a},\rho_{b}].

p3.

\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{a}}(z_{0})}(|\nabla u|^{p}+a(z)|\nabla u|^{q}+b(z)|\nabla u|^{s})dz=\lambda^{p}.

We shall start by proving uniform parabolic Sobolev Poincaré inequalities in
p
-intrinsic cylinders for any
\frac{2n}{n+2}<p\leq q\leq s<\infty
. The following lemma is the first step towards this.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
) and let the
Item

p1.
be in force. Then for any
\theta\in\left(\max\left\{\frac{s-1}{p},\frac{1}{p}\right\},1\right],
there exists a constant
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})
such that the following estimates,

(i)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta p}dz

\displaystyle\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\nabla u\right|^{\theta p}dz+c\lambda^{(2-p)\theta p}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle+c\lambda^{(2-q)\theta p}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{q-1}+c\lambda^{(2-s)\theta p}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{s-1},

(ii)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}a^{\theta}(z)\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta q}dz

\displaystyle\leq c\lambda^{(p-q)\theta}\Bigg[\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\nabla u\right|^{\theta q}dz+\lambda^{(2-p)\theta q}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta q}dz\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle+\lambda^{(2-q)\theta q}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta q}dz\right)^{q-1}

\displaystyle+\lambda^{(2-s)\theta q}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta q}dz\right)^{s-1}\Bigg],

(iii)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}a^{\theta}(z)\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta q}dz

\displaystyle\leq c\lambda^{(p-q)\theta}\Bigg[\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\nabla u\right|^{\theta q}dz+\lambda^{(2-p)\theta q}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{\frac{q(p-1)}{p}}

\displaystyle+\lambda^{(2-p)\theta q}\lambda^{(p-q)\theta}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\inf_{w\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}a(w)^{\theta}|\nabla u|^{\theta q}dz\right)^{q-1}

\displaystyle+\lambda^{(2-p)\theta q}\lambda^{(p-s)\frac{\theta q}{s}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\inf_{w\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}b(w)^{\theta}|\nabla u|^{\theta s}\right)^{\frac{q(s-1)}{s}}\Bigg],

(iv)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}b^{\theta}(z)\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta s}dz

\displaystyle\leq c\lambda^{(p-s)\theta}\Bigg[\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\nabla u\right|^{\theta s}dz+\lambda^{(2-p)\theta s}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta s}dz\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle+\lambda^{(2-q)\theta s}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta s}dz\right)^{q-1}

\displaystyle+\lambda^{(2-s)\theta s}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta s}dz\right)^{s-1}\Bigg],

(v)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}b^{\theta}(z)\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta s}dz

\displaystyle\leq c\lambda^{(p-s)\theta}\Bigg[\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\nabla u\right|^{\theta s}dz+\lambda^{(2-p)\theta s}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{\frac{s(p-1)}{p}}

\displaystyle+\lambda^{(2-p)\theta s}\lambda^{(p-q)\frac{\theta s}{q}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\inf_{w\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}a(w)^{\theta}|\nabla u|^{\theta q}dz\right)^{\frac{s(q-1)}{q}}

\displaystyle+\lambda^{(2-p)\theta s}\lambda^{(p-s)\theta}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\inf_{w\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}b(w)^{\theta}|\nabla u|^{\theta s}dz\right)^{s-1}\Bigg],

hold whenever
{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}.

Proof.

We split the proof into six steps.

Step 1.
In this step, we prove the following estimate.
Claim:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left(|\nabla u|^{p-1}+a(z)|\nabla u|^{q-1}+b(z)|\nabla u|^{s-1}\right)dz\leq\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{p-1}dz+c\lambda^{p-q}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{q-1}dz

(3.2)

\displaystyle+c\lambda^{p-s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{s-1}dz+c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{\frac{p-1}{\theta p}}.

Note that we only need to estimate the term involving
a(z)
and
b(z)
from the left hand side of (
3.2
). We shall begin with the term involving
a(z)
and the estimation of the term involving
b(z)
will be similar.

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}a(z)|\nabla u|^{q-1}dz

\displaystyle\leq\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|a(z)-a(z_{0})||\nabla u|^{q-1}dz+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}a(z_{0})|\nabla u|^{q-1}dz

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{c[a]_{\alpha}\max\left\{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b},\lambda^{\frac{2\mu-p}{2}}\rho_{b}\right\}^{\alpha}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{q-1}dz}_{I}

\displaystyle+K\lambda^{p-q}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{q-1}dz.

In the first inequality, we used Hölder regularity of
a(\cdot)
and in the second inequality we used
p1
of
Item

p1.
.

Case
\max\left\{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b},\lambda^{\frac{2\mu-p}{2}}\rho_{b}\right\}^{\alpha}=\left(\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}\right)^{\alpha}
:
In this case, using Hölder’s inequality we have

(3.3)

\displaystyle I=c[a]_{\alpha}\left(\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}\right)^{\alpha}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{q-1}dz\leq c[a]_{\alpha}\left(\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}\right)^{\alpha}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{\frac{q-1}{\theta p}}.

Note that from
p3
of
Item

p3.
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda^{p}<\frac{K\lambda^{n-(n+2)\mu}\lambda^{p}}{(2[a]_{\alpha})^{\frac{n+2}{\alpha}}\rho_{a}^{n+2}}.

This immediately implies that

(3.4)

\displaystyle\rho_{a}^{\alpha}\lambda^{\alpha\left(\mu-\frac{n}{n+2}\right)}\leq\frac{K^{\frac{\alpha}{n+2}}}{2[a]_{\alpha}}\leq\frac{K}{2[a]_{\alpha}}.

Using the range of
q
from (
1.4
), i.e.,

\displaystyle q\leq p+\frac{2\alpha}{n+2},

we have

\displaystyle\rho_{a}^{\alpha}\lambda^{\left(\mu-\frac{n}{n+2}\right)\alpha}\lambda^{q}\leq\frac{K}{2[a]_{\alpha}}\lambda^{p}\lambda^{\frac{2\alpha}{n+2}}\implies\left(\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{a}\right)^{\alpha}\leq\frac{K}{2[a]_{\alpha}}\lambda^{p-q}.

Moreover, since
\rho_{b}
and
\rho_{a}
are comparable, we deduce that

(3.5)

\displaystyle\left(\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}\right)^{\alpha}\leq 2^{\alpha}\frac{K}{2[a]_{\alpha}}\lambda^{p-q}.

Thus using (
3.5
) in (
3.3
) and for some
\upgamma>0
to be chosen later, we find

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq c\lambda^{p-q}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{\frac{q-1}{\theta p}}=c\lambda^{p-q}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{\frac{\upgamma}{\theta p}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{\frac{q-1-\upgamma}{\theta p}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{H\"{o}lder's inequality}}{\leq}c\lambda^{p-q}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{p}dz\right)^{\frac{\upgamma}{p}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{\frac{q-1-\upgamma}{\theta p}}.

Now using the assumption
p2
of
Item

p1.
on
p
-intrinsic cylinders, we further have

\displaystyle I\leq c\lambda^{p-q}\lambda^{\upgamma}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{\frac{q-1-\gamma}{\theta p}}.

At this point, we choose
\upgamma=q-p>0,
and finally find

(3.6)

\displaystyle I=c[a]_{\alpha}\left(\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}\right)^{\alpha}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{q-1}dz\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{\frac{p-1}{\theta p}}.

Case
\max\left\{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b},\lambda^{\frac{2\mu-p}{2}}\rho_{b}\right\}^{\alpha}=\left(\lambda^{\frac{2\mu-p}{2}}\rho_{b}\right)^{\alpha}
:
In this case, proceeding as above and using the range of
q
form (
1.4
), i.e.,

\displaystyle q\leq p+\alpha\left(\frac{p}{2}-\frac{n}{n+2}\right),

we arrive at the same conclusion as (
3.5
). Hence using this we again obtain (
3.6
). By similar arguments presented above and using the appropriate range of
s
for different cases, one can obtain

(3.7)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}b(z)|\nabla u|^{s-1}\,dz\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}\,dz\right)^{\frac{p-1}{\theta p}}+K\lambda^{p-s}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{s-1}\,dz

The proof of the claim (
3.2
) is finished combining (

)-(
3.7
).

Step 2.
Now choosing
m=p
and applying the estimate from the previous step in
Lemma

2.4
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta p}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz+c\left(\lambda^{2-p}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\tilde{H}(z,\nabla u)\,dz\right)^{\theta p}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz+c\lambda^{(2-p)\theta p}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{p-1}dz\right)^{\theta p}

\displaystyle+c\lambda^{(2-q)\theta p}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{q-1}dz\right)^{\theta p}+c\lambda^{(2-s)\theta p}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{s-1}dz\right)^{\theta p}

(3.8)

\displaystyle+c\lambda^{(2-p)\theta p}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{p-1}.

Note that an application of Hölder’s inequality gives

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{p-1}dz\right)^{\theta p}\lesssim\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{p-1}\,\,\,\text{for}\,\,\,\,\,\frac{p-1}{p}<\theta\leq 1,

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{q-1}dz\right)^{\theta p}\lesssim\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{q-1}\,\,\,\text{for}\,\,\,\,\,\frac{q-1}{p}<\theta\leq 1,

and

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{s-1}dz\right)^{\theta p}\lesssim\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{s-1}\,\,\,\text{for}\,\,\,\,\,\frac{s-1}{p}<\theta\leq 1.

Substituting the above estimates in (

), we get

.

Step 3.
In this step we prove
(ii)
.

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}a(z)^{\theta}\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta q}dz

\displaystyle\leq\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|a(z)-a(z_{0})|^{\theta}\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta q}dz

\displaystyle+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}a(z_{0})^{\theta}\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta q}dz.

Using Hölder regularity of
a(z)
and
p1
of
Item

p1.
, we get

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}a^{\theta}(z)\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta q}dz

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b},\lambda^{\frac{2\mu-p}{2}}\rho_{b}\right\}^{\alpha\theta}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta q}dz

(3.9)

\displaystyle+K\lambda^{(p-q)\theta}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta q}dz.

Moreover, from
Step 1
we have

(3.10)

\displaystyle\max\left\{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b},\lambda^{\frac{2\mu-p}{2}}\rho_{b}\right\}^{\alpha\theta}\leq c\lambda^{(p-q)\theta}\,\,\,\,\text{for}\,\,\,\,\frac{2n}{n+2}<p<\infty.

Now choosing
m=q
in

2.4
and using the estimate from
Step 1
we get

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta q}dz

(3.11)

\displaystyle\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta q}dz+c\left(\lambda^{2-p}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left(|\nabla u|^{p-1}+a(z)|\nabla u|^{q-1}+b(z)|\nabla u|^{s-1}\right)dz\right)^{\theta q}

\displaystyle\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta q}dz+c\lambda^{(2-p)\theta q}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{p-1}dz\right)^{\theta q}

\displaystyle+c\lambda^{(2-q)\theta q}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{q-1}dz\right)^{\theta q}+c\lambda^{(2-s)\theta q}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{s-1}dz\right)^{\theta q}

(3.12)

\displaystyle+c\lambda^{(2-p)\theta q}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta p}dz\right)^{\frac{(p-1)q}{p}}.

Using the Hölder inequality in each term on the right hand side of the above inequality, we get

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{p-1}dz\right)^{\theta q}\lesssim\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta q}dz\right)^{p-1}\,\,\,\,\text{for}\,\,\,\,\,\,\frac{p-1}{q}<\theta\leq 1,
