Next, we will show that
(v)
cannot hold. We need to show three cases: (
4.11
) and (
4.15
) cannot coexist, (
4.13
) and (
4.17
) cannot coexist, and (
4.11
), (
4.13
), (
4.21
) and (
4.19
) cannot coexist. We start with the first case. We consider the following two subcases.

Case
\max\left\{(\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{\tilde{z}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}})^{\alpha},\left(\frac{\lambda^{\mu}_{\tilde{z}}}{\sqrt{\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}+a(\tilde{z})\lambda^{q}_{\tilde{z}}}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}}\right)^{\alpha}\right\}=(\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{\tilde{z}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}})^{\alpha}
:
In this case,
we have

(4.22)

\displaystyle K\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}\leq 2[a]_{\alpha}(\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{\tilde{z}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}})^{\alpha}\lambda^{q}_{\tilde{z}}.

Note that from (
4.7
), we have

\displaystyle\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{\tilde{z}}}_{\uprho_{\tilde{z}}}(\tilde{z})}|\nabla u|^{p}+a(z)|\nabla u|^{q}dz\leq\frac{\lambda^{n-(n+2)\mu}_{\tilde{z}}\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}}{\uprho^{n+2}_{\tilde{z}}|B_{1}|}\iint_{\Omega_{T}}|\nabla u|^{p}+a(z)|\nabla u|^{q}dz<\frac{K\lambda^{n-(n+2)\mu}_{\tilde{z}}\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}}{\uprho^{n+2}_{\tilde{z}}\left(2[a]_{\alpha}\right)^{\frac{n+2}{\alpha}}}.

This gives

\displaystyle\uprho^{\alpha}_{\tilde{z}}<\lambda^{\alpha\left(\frac{n}{n+2}-\mu\right)}_{\tilde{z}}\frac{K^{\frac{\alpha}{n+2}}}{2[a]_{\alpha}}\leq\lambda^{\alpha\left(\frac{n}{n+2}-\mu\right)}_{\tilde{z}}\frac{K}{2[a]_{\alpha}}.

Using the restriction on
q
from (
1.4
), we get

\displaystyle\uprho^{\alpha}_{\tilde{z}}\lambda^{q}_{\tilde{z}}<\lambda^{\alpha\left(\frac{n}{n+2}-\mu\right)}\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}\lambda^{\frac{2\alpha}{n+2}}_{\tilde{z}}\frac{K}{2[a]_{\alpha}}=\lambda^{\alpha(1-\mu)}_{\tilde{z}}\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}\frac{K}{2[a]_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\implies\left(\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{\tilde{z}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}}\right)^{\alpha}<\lambda^{p-q}_{\tilde{z}}\frac{K}{2[a]_{\alpha}}.

Substituting the above estimate in (
4.22
), we obtain

\displaystyle K\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}\leq 2[a]_{\alpha}(\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{\tilde{z}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}})^{\alpha}\lambda^{q}_{\tilde{z}}<2[a]_{\alpha}\lambda^{p-q}_{\tilde{z}}\frac{K}{2[a]_{\alpha}}=K\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}

which is a contradiction.

Case
\max\left\{(\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{\tilde{z}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}})^{\alpha},\left(\frac{\lambda^{\mu}_{\tilde{z}}}{\sqrt{\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}+a(\tilde{z})\lambda^{q}_{\tilde{z}}}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}}\right)^{\alpha}\right\}=\left(\frac{\lambda^{\mu}_{\tilde{z}}}{\sqrt{\Lambda}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}}\right)^{\alpha}
:
In this case, we have

\displaystyle K\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}\leq 2[a]_{\alpha}\left(\frac{\lambda^{\mu}_{\tilde{z}}}{\sqrt{\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}+a(\tilde{z})\lambda^{q}_{\tilde{z}}}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}}\right)^{\alpha}\lambda^{q}_{\tilde{z}}<2[a]_{\alpha}\left(\lambda^{\frac{2\mu-p}{2}}_{\tilde{z}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}}\right)^{\alpha}\lambda^{q}_{\tilde{z}}.

Again using the restriction on
q
from (
1.4
) and (
4.7
), we arrive at a contradiction as before. Thus, (
4.11
) and (
4.15
) cannot hold together. The non-coexistence of other possibilities can be shown in a similar manner.

4.2.
Vitali covering argument

We start by denoting the following cylinders for any
z\in E_{\Lambda,r_{1}},

(4.23)

Q^{\Lambda}_{z}(z)=\begin{cases}{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{z}}_{r_{z}}(z)\,\,\,\,\,\,\text{if}\,\,\,K\lambda^{p}_{z}\geq a(z)\lambda^{q}_{z},\,\,K\lambda^{p}_{z}\geq b(z)\lambda^{s}_{z}\\
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{z}}_{r_{z}}(z)\,\,\,\,\,\,\text{if}\,\,\,K\lambda^{p}_{z}<a(z)\lambda^{q}_{z},\,\,K\lambda^{p}_{z}\geq b(z)\lambda^{s}_{z}\\
\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{z}}_{r_{z}}(z)\,\,\,\,\,\,\text{if}\,\,\,K\lambda^{p}_{z}\geq a(z)\lambda^{q}_{z},\,\,K\lambda^{p}_{z}<b(z)\lambda^{s}_{z}\\
\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{z}}_{r_{z}}(z)\,\,\,\,\,\,\text{if}\,\,\,K\lambda^{p}_{z}<a(z)\lambda^{q}_{z},\,\,K\lambda^{p}_{z}<b(z)\lambda^{s}_{z}\end{cases}

and for
v,w\in E_{\Lambda,r_{1}},
we denote
\Lambda=\lambda^{p}_{i}+a(i)\lambda^{q}_{i}+b(i)\lambda^{s}_{i}
where
i\in\{v,w\}.

Let us consider the collection of cylinders
\mathcal{F}
that covers
E_{\Lambda,r_{1}},
i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}:=\left\{Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)\,\,\big|\,\,w\in E_{\Lambda,r_{1}}\right\}.

Setting
R=\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{2\kappa},
consider the following subcollection

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{j}:=\left\{Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)\in\mathcal{F}\,\,\Big|\,\,\,\,\,\frac{R}{2^{j}}<r_{w}<\frac{R}{2^{j-1}}\right\},\,\,\,\,\,\,j\in\mathds{N}.

We construct disjoint subcollections
\mathcal{I}_{j}\subset\mathcal{F}_{j}
for
j\in\mathds{N}
as follows. Let
\mathcal{I}_{1}
be the maximal disjoint subcollection in
\mathcal{F}_{1}.
We note that from

\displaystyle\lim_{\Lambda\to\infty}\Lambda|E_{\Lambda}|\leq\lim_{\Lambda\to\infty}\iint_{E_{\Lambda}}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz=0,

we have
|E_{\Lambda}|<\infty.
Using
|E_{\Lambda,r_{1}}|\leq|E_{\Lambda}|<\infty
and (
4.9
), we conclude that the number of cylinders in
\mathcal{I}_{1}
is finite. Suppose we have already chosen
\mathcal{I}_{j}\subset\mathcal{F}_{j}
for
j=1,..,k-1.
Then we construct
\mathcal{I}_{k}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{k}=\left\{Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)\in\mathcal{F}_{k}\,\,\Big|\,\,Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)\cap Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\emptyset\,\,\,\text{for every}\,\,\,Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)\in\cup_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathcal{I}_{j}\right\}.

Therefore,

\mathcal{I}=\cup_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{I}_{j}

would be the maximal collection of pairwise disjoint cylinders in
\mathcal{F}.
Now to prove Vitali’s covering lemma, we need to show:

1.

For any
Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)\in\mathcal{F},
there exists
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)\in\mathcal{I}
such that
Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)\cap Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)\neq\emptyset.

2.

There exists a universal constant
\kappa>1
such that
Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)\subset\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v).

To show the first assertion, fix some
Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)\in\mathcal{F}.
Then
Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)\in\mathcal{F}_{j}
for some
j\in\mathds{N}.
Using the maximality of
\mathcal{I}_{j},
we find that there exists
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)\in\cup_{i=1}^{j}\mathcal{I}_{i}
such that
Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)\cap Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)\neq\emptyset.

We note that by the definition of
\mathcal{I}_{i},

i=1,2,...,j,
it can be shown that the radii are comparable, i.e.,

\displaystyle r_{w}\leq 2r_{v},\,\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,r_{v}\leq 2r_{w}.

Now we prove the second assertion, i.e.,
Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)\subset\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)
in the rest of this section.
We consider the following 16 cases:

(i)

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)={\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)={\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v),

(ii)

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)={\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)={\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v),

(iii)

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)={\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v),

(iv)

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)={\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v),

(v)

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)={\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)={\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v),

(vi)

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)={\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)={\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v),

(vii)

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)={\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v),

(viii)

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)={\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v),

(ix)

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v),

(x)

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)={\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v),

(xi)

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)={\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v),

(xii)

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v),

(xiii)

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v),

(xiv)

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)={\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v),

(xv)

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)={\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v),

(xvi)

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).

First, we show that
\lambda_{v}
and
\lambda_{w}
are comparable in intrinsic cylinders. We note that it is enough to prove the comparison of
\lambda_{v},\lambda_{w}
for
(i)
,
(v)
,
(ix)
and
(xiii)
cases. First, we consider the case
(i)
. By the construction of Vitali covering, we already have
{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)\cap{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v)\neq\emptyset
and
\frac{1}{2}r_{v}\leq r_{w}\leq 2r_{v}.

Claim :

\lambda_{v}
and
\lambda_{w}
are comparable, i.e.,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\lambda_{v}\leq\lambda_{w}\leq K\lambda_{v}.

Let
v=(x_{v},t_{v})
and
w=(x_{w},t_{w}).
Using the Hölder continuity of the coefficient
a(z),
we get

\displaystyle|a(v)-a(w)|

\displaystyle\leq[a]_{\alpha}\max\left\{|x_{v}-x_{w}|,|t_{v}-t_{w}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim[a]_{\alpha}\max\left\{2\left(\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{v}+\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{w}\right)r_{w},2\left(\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}+\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{w}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{w}\right\}^{\alpha}.

Let
\lambda_{w}\leq\lambda_{v}
and we claim that
\lambda_{v}\leq K\lambda_{w}.
On the contrary, let
\lambda_{v}>K\lambda_{w}.
Using the assumption
\lambda_{w}\leq\lambda_{v},
we have

\displaystyle|a(v)-a(w)|\lesssim[a]_{\alpha}\max\left\{2\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{w}r_{w},2\lambda^{\frac{2\mu-p}{2}}_{w}r_{w}\right\}^{\alpha}.

Similarly, the Hölder continuity of
b(z)
implies

\displaystyle|b(v)-b(w)|\lesssim[b]_{\beta}\max\left\{2\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{w}r_{w},2\lambda^{\frac{2\mu-p}{2}}_{w}r_{w}\right\}^{\beta}.

We consider the following two cases.

Case
\max\left\{\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{w}r_{w},\lambda^{\frac{2\mu-p}{2}}_{w}r_{w}\right\}^{\alpha}=\left(\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{w}r_{w}\right)^{\alpha}
:
This case corresponds to

\displaystyle|a(v)-a(w)|\lesssim[a]_{\alpha}\left(2\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{w}r_{w}\right)^{\alpha}

and following (
3.4
) we further have

\displaystyle r^{\alpha}_{w}\lambda^{\alpha\left(\mu-\frac{n}{n+2}\right)}_{w}\leq\frac{K}{2[a]_{\alpha}}

and

\displaystyle r^{\beta}_{w}\lambda^{\beta\left(\mu-\frac{n}{n+2}\right)}_{w}\leq\frac{K}{2[b]_{\beta}}.

From the definition of
\Lambda,
we have

\displaystyle\Lambda=\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{p}_{w}+[a]_{\alpha}2^{\alpha}\lambda^{(-1+\mu)\alpha}_{w}r^{\alpha}_{w}\lambda^{q}_{w}

\displaystyle+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{w}+[b]_{\beta}\max\left\{2\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{w}r_{w},2\lambda^{\frac{2\mu-p}{2}}_{w}r_{w}\right\}^{\beta}\lambda^{s}_{w}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{w}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{bounds on maximum radius}}{\leq}\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{w}+K\lambda^{p}_{w}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{w}.

Now substituting
\lambda_{v}>K\lambda_{w}
and
\lambda_{w}\leq\lambda_{v}
in the above estimate, we obtain,

\displaystyle\Lambda<\frac{\lambda^{p}_{v}}{K^{p}}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}+\frac{K\lambda^{p}_{v}}{K^{p}}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{K^{p}}+\frac{1}{K^{p-1}}\right)\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{K^{p-1}}\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}<\Lambda=\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}

since
K>2^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.
This gives a contradiction.

Case
\max\left\{\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{w}r_{w},\lambda^{\frac{2\mu-p}{2}}_{w}r_{w}\right\}^{\alpha}=\left(\lambda^{\frac{2\mu-p}{2}}_{w}r_{w}\right)^{\alpha}
:
This case corresponds to

\displaystyle|a(v)-a(w)|\lesssim[a]_{\alpha}\left(2\lambda^{\frac{2\mu-p}{2}}_{w}r_{w}\right)^{\alpha}

and using the range of
q,
i.e.,

\displaystyle q\leq p+\alpha\left(\frac{p}{2}-\frac{n}{n+2}\right),

we arrive at the same conclusion.

Next, letting
\lambda_{v}\leq\lambda_{w},
in a similar way as above, we can show that
\lambda_{w}\leq K\lambda_{v}
by interchanging the role of
v
and
w
above. The cases
(ii)
,
(iii)
,
(iv)
follow from case
(i)
.

In case
(v)
, let
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)\cap{\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v)\neq\emptyset.
We claim
\frac{1}{K}\lambda_{v}\leq\lambda_{w}\leq K\lambda_{v}.
On the contrary, let us assume that
\lambda_{w}>K\lambda_{v}.
Using (
4.10
) and the Hölder regularity of
b(z),
we obtain

\displaystyle\Lambda=\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}<\frac{\lambda^{p}_{v}}{K^{p}}+\frac{2a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}{K^{q}}+\frac{\lambda^{p}_{v}}{2K^{p-1}}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}

\displaystyle<\left(\frac{1}{K^{p}}+\frac{1}{2K^{p-1}}\right)\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{p}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{K^{p-1}}\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}<\Lambda

which is a contradiction. Similarly, we can show that
\lambda_{v}\leq K\lambda_{w}.
The cases
(vi)
,
(vii)
and
(viii)
follow from the case
(v)
.

Now we consider case
(ix)
. Let
\tilde{z}\in\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)\cap\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).
In this case, we can use
\frac{b(\tilde{z})}{2}\leq b(w)\leq 2b(\tilde{z})
and Hölder regularity of
a(z)
to conclude that
\frac{1}{K}\lambda_{v}\leq\lambda_{w}\leq K\lambda_{v}.
Furthermore, the cases
(x)
,
(xi)
and
(xii)
follow from
(ix)
.

In the case
(xiii)
, for
\tilde{z}\in\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)\cap\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v),
we can use
\frac{b(\tilde{z})}{2}\leq b(w)\leq 2b(\tilde{z})
and
\frac{a(\tilde{z})}{2}\leq a(w)\leq 2a(\tilde{z})
to conclude the same. The rest of the cases follows from
(xiii)
.

Space inclusion.
Let
w=(w_{0},t_{w})
and
v=(v_{0},t_{v}).
By considering the space part, we have

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=B_{\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{w}r_{w}}(w_{0})\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=B_{\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}}(v_{0}).

First we note that

B_{\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{w}r_{w}}(w_{0})\cap B_{\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{v}r_{v}}(v_{0})\neq\phi.

Let
x\in B_{\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{w}r_{w}}(w_{0}).
Then

|x-v_{0}|\leq|x-w_{0}|+|w_{0}-v_{0}|\leq 2\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{w}r_{w}+\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{v}r_{v}.

Using
K^{-1}\lambda_{w}\leq\lambda_{v}\leq K\lambda_{w}
and
r_{w}\leq 2r_{v},
we conclude that the trailing inequality

\displaystyle 2\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{w}r_{w}+\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{v}r_{v}\leq\left(K^{1-\mu}+1\right)\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{v}r_{v}\leq\kappa\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{v}r_{v}

holds if

\displaystyle\kappa\geq 1+4K^{1-\mu}.

Time inclusion.
Now we will show that the inclusion of the time intervals hold in each case.

Situation
(i)
:

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)={\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)={\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).
Considering the time part only, we have

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=I^{\lambda_{w},p}_{r_{w}}(t_{w}):=\left(t_{w}-\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{w}r_{w}^{2},t_{w}+\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{w}r^{2}_{w}\right)

\displaystyle\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=I^{\lambda_{v},p}_{\kappa r_{v}}(t_{v}):=\left(t_{v}-\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2},t_{v}+\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}\right).

Let
t\in I^{\lambda_{w},p}_{r_{w}}(t_{w}).
Then

\displaystyle|t-t_{v}|\leq|t-t_{w}|+|t_{v}-t_{w}|\leq 2\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{w}r^{2}_{w}+\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}r^{2}_{v}

\displaystyle\leq 8K^{p-2\mu}\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}r^{2}_{v}+\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}r^{2}_{v}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}r_{v}^{2}

if

\displaystyle\kappa\geq 1+8K^{p-2\mu}.

Situation
(ii)
:

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)={\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)={\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).
Considering the time intervals,

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=I^{\lambda_{w},p}_{r_{w}}(t_{w}):=\left(t_{w}-\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{w}r^{2}_{w},t_{w}+\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{w}r^{2}_{w}\right)

\displaystyle\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=I^{\lambda_{v},(p,q)}_{\kappa r_{v}}(t_{v})=\left(t_{v}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}},t_{v}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}\right).

Let
t\in I^{\lambda_{w},p}_{r_{w}}(t_{w}).
Then

\displaystyle|t-t_{v}|\leq|t-t_{w}|+|t_{w}-t_{v}|\leq 2\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{w}r^{2}_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}r^{2}_{v}

Note that, in this case we have
K\lambda^{p}_{w}\geq a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}.
This implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{p}_{w}}\leq\frac{1+K}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}}.

Therefore we have

(4.24)

\displaystyle\frac{2\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}r^{2}_{v}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2(1+K)\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}r^{2}_{v}.

Now we claim

\displaystyle\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}\lesssim\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}.

We use
\frac{1}{K}\lambda_{w}\leq\lambda_{v}\leq K\lambda_{w}
and Hölder continuity of
a(z):

\displaystyle\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}

\displaystyle\leq K^{p}\lambda^{p}_{w}+|a(v)-a(w)|\lambda^{q}_{v}+a(w)K^{q}\lambda^{q}_{w}

\displaystyle\leq K^{p}\lambda^{p}_{w}+K\lambda^{p-q}_{v}\lambda^{q}_{v}+a(w)K^{q}\lambda^{q}_{w}

\displaystyle\leq(1+K)K^{p}\lambda^{p}_{w}+K^{q}a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}

\displaystyle\leq(1+K)K^{q}\left(\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}\right).

Now from (
4.24
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{2\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}r^{2}_{v}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2(1+K)\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}r^{2}_{v}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2(1+K)^{2}K^{q}\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}r^{2}_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}r^{2}_{v}

\displaystyle=\left(8(1+K)^{2}K^{q+2\mu}+1\right)\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}r^{2}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}\leq\kappa\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}r^{2}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}

if

\displaystyle\kappa\geq\left(8(1+K)^{2}K^{q+2\mu}+1\right).

Situation
(iii)
:

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)={\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=I^{\lambda_{w},p}_{r_{w}}(t_{w}):=\left(t_{w}-\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{w}r^{2}_{w},t_{w}+\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{w}r^{2}_{w}\right)

\displaystyle\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=I^{\lambda_{v},(p,s)}_{\kappa r_{v}}(t_{v})=\left(t_{v}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}},t_{v}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}}\right).

Now using
p1
of
Item

p1.
and following the same calculation as above, we find that
Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)\subset\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)
if
k\geq\left(8(1+K)^{2}K^{s+2\mu}+1\right).

Situation
(iv)
:

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)={\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=I^{\lambda_{w},p}_{r_{w}}(t_{w}):=\left(t_{w}-\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{w}r^{2}_{w},t_{w}+\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{w}r^{2}_{w}\right)

\displaystyle\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=I^{\lambda_{v},(p,q,s)}_{\kappa r_{v}}(t_{v})=\left(t_{v}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}},t_{v}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}}\right).

Following the same calculation as previous and using Hölder regularity of
a(z)
and
b(z)
, we find that
Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)\subset\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)
if
k\geq\left(8(2+2K)^{2}K^{s+2\mu}+1\right).

Situation
(v)
:

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)={\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)={\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).
Considering the time part, we have

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=I^{\lambda_{w},(p,q)}_{r_{w}}(t_{w})=\left(t_{w}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}}r^{2}_{w},t_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}\right)

\displaystyle\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=I^{\lambda_{v},(p,q)}_{\kappa r_{v}}(t_{v})=\left(t_{v}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}},t_{v}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}\right).

Let
t\in I^{\lambda_{w},(p,q)}_{r_{w}}(t_{w}).
Then

\displaystyle|t-t_{v}|\leq|t-t_{w}|+|t_{v}-t_{w}|\leq\frac{2\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}r^{2}_{w}

Note that, in situation
(ii)
, we already proved
\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}\leq(1+K)K^{q}(\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w})
and we may use it here since
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)\subset{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w).
This gives

\displaystyle|t-t_{v}|\leq\frac{2\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}r^{2}_{v}

\displaystyle=\left(8(1+K)K^{q+2\mu}+1\right)\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}r^{2}_{v}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}r^{2}_{v}

if
\kappa>\left(8(1+K)K^{q+2\mu}+1\right).

Situation
(vi)
:

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)={\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)={\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).
We consider the time intervals, i.e.,

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=I^{\lambda_{w},(p,q)}_{r_{w}}(t_{w})=\left(t_{w}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}}r^{2}_{w},t_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=I^{\lambda_{v},p}_{\kappa r_{v}}(t_{v})=\left(t_{v}-\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2},\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}\right).

Let
t\in I^{\lambda_{w},(p,q)}_{r_{w}}(t_{w}).
Then

\displaystyle|t-t_{v}|\leq|t-t_{w}|+|t_{w}-t_{v}|\leq\frac{2\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}+\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}r^{2}_{v}.

Now using
K\lambda^{p}_{w}\leq a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w},
we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{2\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}+\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}r^{2}_{v}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{(1+K)\lambda^{p}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}+\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}r^{2}_{v}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{1+K}\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{w}r^{2}_{w}+\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}r^{2}_{v}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{1+K}K^{p-2\mu}\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}r^{2}_{v}+\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}r^{2}_{v}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}r_{v}^{2}

if

\displaystyle\kappa\geq\frac{8}{1+K}K^{p-2\mu}+1.

Situation
(vii)
:

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)={\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).
We consider the time intervals, i.e.,

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=I^{\lambda_{w},(p,q)}_{r_{w}}(t_{w})=\left(t_{w}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}}r^{2}_{w},t_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=I^{\lambda_{v},(p,s)}_{\kappa r_{v}}(t_{v})=\left(t_{v}-\frac{\kappa\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}}r^{2}_{v},t_{v}+\frac{\kappa\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}}r^{2}_{v}\right).

Let
t\in I^{\lambda_{w},(p,q)}_{r_{w}}(t_{w}).
Then, using
pq1
of
Item

pq1.
and
ps1
of
Item

ps1.
, we get

\displaystyle|t-t_{v}|\leq|t-t_{w}|+|t_{w}-t_{v}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}}r^{2}_{v}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}}r^{2}_{v}.

Again, following the calculations as above, we have
\lambda^{p}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}\leq(1+K)K^{s}(\lambda^{p}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w})
and we conclude that
Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)\subset\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)
if
k\geq 8(1+K)^{2}K^{s+2\mu}+1.

Situation
(viii)
:

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)={\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).
We consider the time intervals, i.e.,

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=I^{\lambda_{w},(p,q)}_{r_{w}}(t_{w})=\left(t_{w}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}}r^{2}_{w},t_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=I^{\lambda_{v},(p,q,s)}_{\kappa r_{v}}(t_{v})=\left(t_{v}-\frac{\kappa\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}}r^{2}_{v},t_{v}+\frac{\kappa\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}}r^{2}_{v}\right).

This situation is similar to situation
(iv)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)\subset\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)
if
k\geq\left(8(2+K)^{2}K^{s+2\mu}+1\right).

Situation
(ix)
:

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).
We consider the time intervals, i.e.,

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=I^{\lambda_{w},(p,s)}_{r_{w}}(t_{w})=\left(t_{w}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w},t_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=I^{\lambda_{v},(p,s)}_{\kappa r_{v}}(t_{v})=\left(t_{v}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}},t_{v}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}}\right).

This situation is the same as the situation
(vii)
with
\kappa\geq 8(1+K)^{2}K^{s+2\mu}+1.

Situation
(x)
:

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{P}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).
We consider the time intervals, i.e.,

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=I^{\lambda_{w},(p,s)}_{r_{w}}(t_{w})=\left(t_{w}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w},t_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=I^{\lambda_{v},p}_{\kappa r_{v}}(t_{v})=\left(t_{v}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}},t_{v}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}}\right).

This situation is the same as the situation
(i)
with
\kappa\geq 1+8K^{p-2\mu}.

Situation
(xi)
:

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{Q}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).
We consider the time intervals, i.e.,

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=I^{\lambda_{w},(p,s)}_{r_{w}}(t_{w})=\left(t_{w}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w},t_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=I^{\lambda_{v},(p,q)}_{\kappa r_{v}}(t_{v})=\left(t_{v}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}},t_{v}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}\right).

This situation is the same as the situation
(vii)
with
\kappa\geq 8(1+K)^{2}K^{q+2\mu}+1.

Situation
(xii)
:

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).
We consider the time intervals, i.e.,

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=I^{\lambda_{w},(p,s)}_{r_{w}}(t_{w})=\left(t_{w}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w},t_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=I^{\lambda_{v},(p,q,s)}_{\kappa r_{v}}(t_{v})=\left(t_{v}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}},t_{v}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}}\right).

This situation is same as situation
(viii)
with
\kappa\geq 8(2+K)^{2}K^{s+2\mu}+1.

Situation
(xiii)
:

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).
We consider the time intervals, i.e.,

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=I^{\lambda_{w},(p,q,s)}_{r_{w}}(t_{w})=\left(t_{w}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w},t_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=I^{\lambda_{v},(p,q,s)}_{\kappa r_{v}}(t_{v})=\left(t_{v}-\frac{\kappa\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}}r^{2}_{v},t_{v}+\frac{\kappa\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}}r^{2}_{v}\right).

This situation is similar to the situation
(v)
. Indeed, using Hölder regularity of
a(z)
and
b(z),
one can show

\displaystyle\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}\leq(2+K)K^{s}(\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w})

Following the calculations of situation
(v)
, we find
Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)\subset\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)
if
k\geq 8(2+K)K^{s+2\mu}+1.

Situation
(xiv)
:

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{P}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).
We consider the time intervals, i.e.,

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=I^{\lambda_{w},(p,q,s)}_{r_{w}}(t_{w})=\left(t_{w}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w},t_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=I^{\lambda_{v},(p)}_{\kappa r_{v}}(t_{v})=\left(t_{v}-\kappa\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}r^{2}_{v},t_{v}+\kappa\lambda^{2\mu-p}_{v}r^{2}_{v}\right).

This situation can be concluded from the situation
(i)
with
\kappa\geq 1+8K^{p-2\mu}

Situation
(xv)
:

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{Q}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).
We consider the time intervals, i.e.,

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=I^{\lambda_{w},(p,q,s)}_{r_{w}}(t_{w})=\left(t_{w}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w},t_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=I^{\lambda_{v},(p,q)}_{\kappa r_{v}}(t_{v})=\left(t_{v}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}},t_{v}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+a(v)\lambda^{q}_{v}}\right).

This situation is the same as situation
(v)
with
\kappa\geq\left(8(1+K)K^{q+2\mu}+1\right).

Situation
(xvi)
:

Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=\mathcal{G}^{\lambda_{w}}_{r_{w}}(w)
and
Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=\mathcal{S}^{\lambda_{v}}_{r_{v}}(v).
We consider the time intervals, i.e.,

\displaystyle Q^{\Lambda}_{w}(w)=I^{\lambda_{w},(p,q,s)}_{r_{w}}(t_{w})=\left(t_{w}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w},t_{w}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{w}}{\lambda^{p}_{w}+a(w)\lambda^{q}_{w}+b(w)\lambda^{s}_{w}}r^{2}_{w}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{v}(v)=I^{\lambda_{v},(p,s)}_{\kappa r_{v}}(t_{v})=\left(t_{v}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}},t_{v}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}_{v}\kappa r_{v}^{2}}{\lambda^{p}_{v}+b(v)\lambda^{s}_{v}}\right).

This situation is the same as situation
(v)
with
\kappa\geq\left(8(1+K)K^{q+2\mu}+1\right)
.

Hence, combining all the above cases, it is enough to take

(4.25)

\displaystyle\kappa:=32(1+K)^{2}K^{s+2\mu}+1.

This completes the proof of 2, and hence the covering argument. We summarize the above content in the following lemma.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
\kappa
be as in (
4.25
) and
E_{\Lambda,r_{1}}
as in (
4.2
). There exists a collection
\{Q^{\Lambda}_{z_{i}}\}_{i\in\mathds{N}}
of cylinders defined in (
4.23
) that satisfies the following.

(i)

\cup_{i\in\mathds{N}}\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{z_{i}}=E_{\Lambda,r_{1}}
.

(ii)

Q^{\Lambda}_{z_{i}}\cap Q^{\Lambda}_{z_{j}}=\emptyset
for every
i,j\in\mathds{N}
with
i\neq j
.

4.3.
Proof of main theorem

In this section, we complete the proof of
Theorem

1.1
. First, we derive the following lemma as a consequence of reverse Hölder inequalities.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
). Then there exist constants
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})
and
\theta_{0}\in(0,1)
such that for any
\theta\in(\theta_{0},1),
we have

(i)

\displaystyle\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\kappa\varrho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz\leq c\Lambda^{1-\theta}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})\cap E_{\Lambda/c}}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz,

(ii)

\displaystyle\iint_{{\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda}_{2\kappa\varrho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz\leq c\Lambda^{1-\theta}\iint_{{\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})\cap E_{\Lambda/c}}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz,

(iii)

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{S}^{\lambda}_{2\kappa\varrho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz\leq c\Lambda^{1-\theta}\iint_{\mathcal{S}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})\cap E_{\Lambda/c}}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz,

(iv)

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{G}^{\lambda}_{2\kappa\varrho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz\leq c\Lambda^{1-\theta}\iint_{\mathcal{G}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})\cap E_{\Lambda/c}}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz,

whenever
{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0}),\,{\mathcal{Q}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0}),\mathcal{S}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0}),\mathcal{G}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}.

Proof.

First we note that using
p2
of
Item

p3.
, we get

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}\,dz\right)^{\frac{1-\theta}{\theta}}\leq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz\right)^{1-\theta}<\lambda^{p(1-\theta)}.

Hence, we have

(4.26)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}\,dz\right)^{\frac{1}{\theta}}\leq\lambda^{p(1-\theta)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}\,dz.

Now we write

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})=\left({\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}\cap E_{\frac{\lambda^{p}}{(2c)^{1/\theta}}}\right)\cup\left({\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}\setminus E_{\frac{\lambda^{p}}{(2c)^{1/\theta}}}\right).

From the definition of
E_{\Lambda}
given in (
4.2
), the right hand side of (
4.26
) can be estimated as

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}\,dz\right)^{\frac{1}{\theta}}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{p(1-\theta)}\frac{\lambda^{p\theta}}{2c}\frac{\left|{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}\setminus E_{\frac{\lambda^{p}}{(2c)^{1/\theta}}}\right|}{\left|{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}\right|}+\frac{\lambda^{p(1-\theta)}}{\left|{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}\right|}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}\cap E_{\frac{\lambda^{p}}{(2c)^{1/\theta}}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}\,dz

(4.27)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda^{p}}{2c}+\frac{\lambda^{p(1-\theta)}}{\left|{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}\right|}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}\cap E_{\frac{\lambda^{p}}{(2c)^{1/\theta}}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}\,dz.

Now from the reverse Hölder inequality (
Lemma

3.7
) and (
4.27
), we obtain that

(4.28)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}\,dz\right)^{\frac{1}{\theta}}\leq\frac{\lambda^{p}}{2}+\frac{c\lambda^{p(1-\theta)}}{\left|{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}\right|}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}\cap E_{\frac{\lambda^{p}}{(2c)^{1/\theta}}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}\,dz.

Now we choose
\rho_{a}=\varrho,
and from
p3
of
Item

p3.
we have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz=\lambda^{p}.

Therefore from (
4.28
) and
Item

p2.
we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz<\frac{\lambda^{p}}{2}\leq\frac{c\lambda^{p(1-\theta)}}{\left|{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}\right|}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}\cap E_{\frac{\lambda^{p}}{(2c)^{1/\theta}}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz.

This implies

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz<\frac{2c\lambda^{p(1-\theta)}}{\left|{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}\right|}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}\cap E_{\frac{\lambda^{p}}{(2c)^{1/\theta}}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}\,dz.

Now we choose
\rho_{b}=2\kappa\varrho
to get

(4.29)

\displaystyle\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\kappa\varrho}}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz\leq 2c\kappa^{n+2}\lambda^{p(1-\theta)}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}\cap E_{\frac{\lambda^{p}}{(2c)^{1/\theta}}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz.

Using
K\lambda^{p}\geq\frac{a(z_{0})}{K}\lambda^{q},
we get
\lambda^{p}\geq\frac{\lambda^{p}+a(z_{0})\lambda^{q}}{2K}=\frac{\Lambda}{2K}.
Hence we have
\frac{\lambda^{p}}{(2c)^{1/\theta}}\geq\frac{\lambda^{p}}{(2c)^{1/\theta_{0}}}\geq\frac{\Lambda}{2K(2c)^{1/\theta_{0}}}.
This gives that
E_{\frac{\lambda^{p}}{(2c)^{1/\theta}}}\subset E_{\frac{\Lambda}{2K(2c)^{1/\theta_{0}}}}.
Now by setting
2K(2c)^{1/\theta_{0}}=c,
from (
4.29
) we obtain

\displaystyle\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\kappa\varrho}}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz\leq c\Lambda^{1-\theta}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}\cap E_{\Lambda/c}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz.

The same proof works for the
(p,q),(p,s)
and
(p,q,s)
-intrinsic cylinders also. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.1
.

Observe that in
Definition

1.3
, we may consider
\rho_{b}\in[2\varrho,4\kappa\varrho],
making the above selection of
\rho_{b}
admissible.

Now we are ready to prove
Theorem

1.1
. We present a detailed proof for the sake of completeness.

Proof of
Theorem

1.1
.

Following
Proposition

4.1
, we have a countable pairwise disjoint collection
\mathcal{I}:=\left\{Q^{\Lambda}_{z_{j}}\right\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
for
z_{j}\in E_{\Lambda,r_{1}}.
From the previous
Lemma

4.1
, there exists constants
c
and
\theta_{0}\in(0,1)
we have

\displaystyle\iint_{\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{z_{j}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz\leq c\Lambda^{1-\theta}\iint_{Q^{\Lambda}_{z_{j}}\cap E_{\frac{\Lambda}{c}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}\,dz

for every
j\in\mathds{N}
and
\theta\in(\theta_{0},1).
Since the cylinders in
\mathcal{I}
are pairwise disjoint, we get

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{\Lambda.r_{1}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\iint_{\kappa Q^{\Lambda}_{z_{j}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c\Lambda^{1-\theta}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\iint_{Q^{\Lambda}_{z_{j}}\cap E_{\frac{\Lambda}{c}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz

(4.30)

\displaystyle\leq c\Lambda^{1-\theta}\iint_{E_{\frac{\Lambda}{c},r_{2}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz.

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{\frac{\Lambda}{c},r_{1}}\setminus E_{\Lambda,r_{1}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz

\displaystyle=\iint_{E_{\frac{\Lambda}{c},r_{1}}\setminus E_{\Lambda,r_{1}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{1-\theta}\,dz

(4.31)

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{1-\theta}\iint_{E_{\frac{\Lambda}{c}},r_{2}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}\,dz.

Now combining (
4.30
) and (
4.31
) we conclude that

\displaystyle\iint_{E_{\frac{\Lambda}{c},r_{1}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)dz

\displaystyle=\iint_{E_{\Lambda,r_{1}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)dz+\iint_{E_{\frac{\Lambda}{c},r_{1}}\setminus E_{\Lambda,r_{1}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)dz

(4.32)

\displaystyle\leq c\Lambda^{1-\theta}\iint_{E_{\frac{\Lambda}{c},r_{2}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz.

For any
k\in\mathds{N},
we define the truncation operator as

\displaystyle H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}=\min\left\{H(z,|\nabla u|),k\right\}

and the superlevel set as

\displaystyle E^{k}_{\Lambda,\uprho}:=\left\{z\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\uprho}(z_{0})\,\,\Big|\,\,H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}>\Lambda\right\}.

From the above definition, we clearly have

(4.33)

\displaystyle E^{k}_{\Lambda,\uprho}=\begin{cases}E_{\Lambda,\uprho}\,\,\,\,&\text{if}\,\,\,\Lambda\leq k\\
\emptyset\,\,\,\,&\text{if}\,\,\,\Lambda>k.\end{cases}

Therefore from (
4.32
) and (
4.33
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\iint_{E^{k}_{\frac{\Lambda}{c},r_{1}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz\leq\iint_{E_{\frac{\Lambda}{c},r_{1}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)dz

\displaystyle\leq c\Lambda^{1-\theta}\iint_{E_{\frac{\Lambda}{c},r_{2}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz

\displaystyle\leq c\Lambda^{1-\theta}\iint_{E^{k}_{\frac{\Lambda}{c},r_{2}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz.

Recalling (
4.3
) we denote

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}=\frac{1}{c}\left(\frac{4\kappa r}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{\frac{s(n+2)}{(n+2)\mu-n}}\Lambda_{0}.

Then for any
\Lambda>\Lambda_{1},
we have

(4.34)

\displaystyle\iint_{E^{k}_{\Lambda,r_{1}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz\leq c\Lambda^{1-\theta}\iint_{E^{k}_{\Lambda,r_{2}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
to be determined later. Multiplying the above inequality (
4.34
) with
\Lambda^{\varepsilon-1}
and integrating over
(\Lambda_{1},\infty),
we obtain

(4.35)

\displaystyle\int_{\Lambda_{1}}^{\infty}\Lambda^{\varepsilon-1}\iint_{E^{k}_{\Lambda,r_{1}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dzd\Lambda\leq c\int_{\Lambda_{1}}^{\infty}\Lambda^{\varepsilon-\theta}\iint_{E^{k}_{\Lambda,r_{2}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dzd\Lambda.

Now using Fubini’s theorem on the left hand side of the above inequality (
4.35
), we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Lambda_{1}}^{\infty}\Lambda^{\varepsilon-1}\iint_{E^{k}_{\Lambda,r_{1}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dzd\Lambda

\displaystyle=\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{r_{1}}(z_{0})}\int_{\Lambda_{1}}^{H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}}\Lambda^{\varepsilon-1}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}d\Lambda dz

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{r_{1}}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta+\varepsilon}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz-\frac{\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{1}}{\varepsilon}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{r_{1}}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{r_{1}}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta+\varepsilon}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz-\frac{\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{1}}{\varepsilon}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{2r}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz.

Similarly applying Fubini’s theorem on the right hand side of (
4.35
), we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Lambda_{1}}^{\infty}\Lambda^{\varepsilon-\theta}\iint_{E^{k}_{\Lambda,r_{2}}}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dzd\Lambda=\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{r_{2}}(z_{0})}\int_{\Lambda_{1}}^{H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}}\Lambda^{\varepsilon-\theta}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}d\Lambda dz

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon-\theta}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{r_{2}}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta+\varepsilon}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz-\frac{\Lambda^{1+\varepsilon-\theta}}{1+\varepsilon-\theta}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{r_{2}}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon-\theta}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{r_{2}}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta+\varepsilon}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz.

Plugging the above estimates in (
4.35
), we obtain

\displaystyle\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{r_{1}}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta+\varepsilon}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{1}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{2r}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz

\displaystyle+\frac{c\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon-\theta}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{r_{2}}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta+\varepsilon}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz.

Now we choose a small
\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}<1
such that
\frac{c\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon-\theta}\leq\frac{1}{2}.
Now we aim to use
Lemma

2.2
. For that purpose, we define

\displaystyle h(r)=\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{r}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta+\varepsilon}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz.

Considering the choice of
\Lambda_{1},
we have

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{1}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{2r}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{\frac{\varepsilon s(n+2)}{(n+2)\mu-n}}}\frac{(4\kappa r)^{\frac{\varepsilon s(n+2)}{(n+2)\mu-n}}}{c^{\varepsilon}}\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{0}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{2r}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz

\displaystyle:=\frac{A}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{\frac{\varepsilon s(n+2)}{(n+2)\mu-n}}}.

Now applying
Lemma

2.2
with
\varrho_{1}=r_{1},\varrho_{2}=r_{2},R=2r,\vartheta=\frac{c\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon-\theta},B=0
and
\gamma_{1}=\frac{\varepsilon q(n+2)}{(n+2)\mu-n},
we obtain

\displaystyle\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{r}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta+\varepsilon}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz

\displaystyle\lesssim_{(\vartheta,\gamma_{1})}\frac{1}{r^{\frac{\varepsilon q(n+2)}{(n+2)\mu-n}}}\frac{(4\kappa r)^{\frac{\varepsilon q(n+2)}{(n+2)\mu-n}}}{c^{\varepsilon}}\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{0}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{2r}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)_{k}^{1-\theta}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{\theta}dz.

Letting
k\to\infty,
we finally obtain

\displaystyle\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{r}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{1+\varepsilon}\,dz\leq c\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{0}\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{2r}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz.

Taking care of
\Lambda^{\varepsilon}_{0}
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{r}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{1+\varepsilon}\,dz\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{2r}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\right)^{1+\frac{\varepsilon s}{(n+2)\mu-n}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

5.
Remarks on general multi-phase problem

In this section, we present a brief discussion on the parabolic multi-phase problem with finitely many phases, omitting detailed proofs for brevity. To be more precise, we wish to deal with the system:

(5.1)

\displaystyle u_{t}-\operatorname*{div}\left(|\nabla u|^{p-2}\nabla u+\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}(z)|\nabla u|^{p_{i}-2}\nabla u\right)=0

where
\frac{2n}{n+2}<p\leq p_{1}\leq\cdots\leq p_{m}<\infty
and
0\leq a_{i}(\cdot)\in C^{0,\alpha_{i}}(\Omega_{T})
with
\alpha_{i}\in(0,1]
for all
i\in\{1,\cdots,m\}.
As previous, we also assume

\displaystyle\frac{2n}{n+2}<p\leq p_{i}\leq p+\min\left\{\alpha_{i}\left(\frac{p}{2}-\frac{n}{n+2}\right),\frac{2\alpha_{i}}{n+2}\right\}<\infty\,\,\,\text{for all}\,\,\,i\in\{1,\cdots,m\}.

Moreover, we denote

(5.2)

\displaystyle H(z,|\nabla u|):=|\nabla u|^{p}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}(z)|\nabla u|^{p_{i}},

(5.3)

\displaystyle\tilde{H}(z,|\nabla u|):=|\nabla u|^{p-1}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}(z)|\nabla u|^{p_{i}-1},

With these notations in mind, we can derive a
Caccioppoli inequality
following
[
21
, Lemma 3.1]
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
5.1
). Then there exists a constant
c=c(n,p,p_{1},\cdots,p_{m})
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in l_{S_{2}}(t_{0})}\fint_{B_{R_{2}}(x_{0})}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{Q_{R_{2},S_{2}}}\right|^{2}}{S_{2}}\,dx+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{R_{2},S_{2}}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{R_{2},S_{2}}(z_{0})}H\left(z,\left|\frac{u-(u)_{Q_{R_{2},S_{2}}}}{R_{2}-R_{1}}\right|\right)\,dx\,dt+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{R_{2},S_{2}}(z_{0})}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{Q_{R_{2},S_{2}}}\right|^{2}}{S_{2}-S_{1}}\,dx\,dt

holds for every
Q_{R_{2},S_{2}}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T},
where
H(z,\cdot)
is given by (
5.2
).

Similarly, one can deduce the parabolic Poincaré inequality as in
[
21
, Lemma 3.3]
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
5.1
). Then there exists a constant
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})
such that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r,\varrho}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{u-(u)_{Q_{r,\varrho}(z_{0})}}{r}\right|^{\theta\upgamma}\,dz\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r,\varrho}(z_{0})}|\nabla u|^{\theta\upgamma}dz+c\left(\frac{\varrho}{r^{2}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{Q_{r,\varrho}(z_{0})}\tilde{H}(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz\right)^{\theta\upgamma}

for any
Q_{r,\varrho}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}
defined in
Section

1.2.1

(iii)
with
\upgamma\in(1,p_{m}]
and
\theta\in\left(\frac{1}{\upgamma},1\right],
where
\tilde{H}(z,\cdot)
is given by (
5.3
).

5.1.
Intrinsic geometry

In this section, we describe the intrinsic geometry governed by equation (
5.1
). We will start by defining the intrinsic cylinders.

5.1.1.
Intrinsic cylinders

In this context, we will have
m+2
types of intrinsic cylinders, namely,

(i)

{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0})
is used to denote
p
-intrinsic cylinders at
z_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0}),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0}):=B_{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\varrho}(x_{0})\times\left(t_{0}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}\varrho^{2}}{\lambda^{p}},t_{0}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}\varrho^{2}}{\lambda^{p}}\right)=:B^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times I^{\lambda,p}_{\varrho}(t_{0}).

(ii)

For all
i\in\{1,\cdots,m\},

{\mathcal{P}}^{i,\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0})
is used to denote
(p,p_{i})
-intrinsic cylinders at
z_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0}),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}^{i,\lambda}_{\varrho}(z_{0}):=B_{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\varrho}(x_{0})\times\left(t_{0}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}\varrho^{2}}{\lambda^{p}+a_{i}(z_{0})\lambda^{p_{i}}},t_{0}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}\varrho^{2}}{\lambda^{p}+a_{i}(z_{0})\lambda^{p_{i}}}\right)=:B^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times I^{\lambda,(p,p_{i})}_{\varrho}(t_{0}).

(iii)

\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})
is used to denote
(p,p_{1},\cdots,p_{m})
-intrinsic cylinders at
z_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0}):

\displaystyle=B_{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\varrho}(x_{0})\times\left(t_{0}-\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}\varrho^{2}}{\lambda^{p}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}(z_{0})\lambda^{p_{i}}},t_{0}+\frac{\lambda^{2\mu}\varrho^{2}}{\lambda^{p}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}(z_{0})\lambda^{p_{i}}}\right)

\displaystyle=:B^{\lambda}_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times I^{\lambda,(p,p_{1},\cdots,p_{m})}_{\varrho}(t_{0}).

5.1.2.
Separation of phases

Here we will describe the separation of the phases. First, we set

\displaystyle K:=2+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\left(2[a_{i}]_{\alpha_{i}}\right)^{\frac{n+2}{\alpha_{i}}}}{|B_{1}|}\iint_{\Omega_{T}}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz.

We shall start with the
p
-phase. We define the
p
-phase as:

(5.4)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}{\bf p1.}\,\,K\lambda^{p}\geq a_{i}(z_{0})\lambda^{p_{i}}\,\,\,\text{for every}\,\,\,i\in\{1,\cdots,m\}.\\
{\bf p2.}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz<\lambda^{p},\,\,\,\text{holds for every}\,\,\,\rho\in(\rho_{a},\rho_{b}].\\
{\bf p3.}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{a}}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz=\lambda^{p}.\end{cases}

Next, we define the
(p,p_{i})
-phase as:

(5.5)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}{\bf ppi1.}\,\,K\lambda^{p}<a_{i}(z_{0})\lambda^{p_{i}},\,\,K\lambda^{p}\geq a_{j}(z_{0})\lambda^{p_{j}}\,\,\,\text{for all}\,\,\,i,j\in\{1,\cdots,m\}\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,i\neq j.\\
{\bf ppi2.}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{i,\lambda}_{\rho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz<\lambda^{p}+a_{i}(z_{0})\lambda^{p_{i}},\,\,\,\text{holds for all}\,\,\,\rho\in(\rho_{a},\rho_{b}].\\
{\bf ppi3.}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{i,\lambda}_{\rho_{a}}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz=\lambda^{p}+a_{i}(z_{0})\lambda^{p_{i}}.\\
{\bf ppi4.}\frac{a_{i}(z_{0})}{2}\leq a_{i}(z)\leq 2a_{i}(z_{0})\,\,\,\text{holds for every}\,\,\,z\in{\mathcal{P}}^{i,\lambda}_{\rho_{a}}(z_{0}).\end{cases}

Finally, we define the
(p,p_{1},\cdots,p_{m})
-phase as:

(5.6)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}{\bf pm1.}\,\,K\lambda^{p}<a_{i}(z_{0})\lambda^{p_{i}},\,\,\text{for every}\,\,\,i\in\{1,\cdots,m\}.\\
{\bf pm2.}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{\rho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz<\lambda^{p}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}(z_{0})\lambda^{p_{i}},\,\,\,\text{holds for all}\,\,\,\rho\in(\rho_{a},\rho_{b}].\\
{\bf pm3.}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{a}}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz=\lambda^{p}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}(z_{0})\lambda^{p_{i}}.\\
{\bf pm4.}\frac{a_{i}(z_{0})}{2}\leq a_{i}(z)\leq 2a_{i}(z_{0})\,\,\,\text{holds for every}\,\,\,z\in\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{a}}(z_{0})\,\,\,\text{and for every}\,\,\,i\in\{1,\cdots,m\}.\end{cases}

5.2.
Parabolic Sobolev-Poincaré inequalities

In this section, we state the parabolic Sobolev-Poincaré inequalities for each of the phases. The proofs can be completed applying the classical Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality as in
Section

3
.

5.2.1.
p
-phase

We state here the parabolic Sobolev-Poincaré inequalities for the
p
-phase, analogous to
Lemma

3.2
. The first step in proving these inequalities involves obtaining an analogue of
Lemma

3.1
. Due to the similarity and length of the proofs, we omit them here.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
5.1
) and let the assumption (
5.4
) be in force. Then for any
\theta\in\left(\max\left\{\frac{p_{m}-1}{p},\frac{1}{p}\right\},1\right]
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1),
there exists a constant
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})
such that the following estimates

(i)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta p}dz\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\nabla u\right|^{\theta p}dz+\varepsilon\lambda^{\theta p},

(ii)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}

\displaystyle\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta p_{i}}dz\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\nabla u\right|^{\theta p_{i}}dz+c\lambda^{(p_{i}-p)\theta}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}a_{i}^{\theta}(z)|\nabla u|^{\theta p_{i}}dz\right)

\displaystyle+c\underset{i\neq j}{\sum_{{j=1}}^{m}}\lambda^{(2-p)\theta p_{i}}\lambda^{(p-p_{j})\frac{\theta p_{i}}{p_{j}}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\inf_{z\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}a^{\theta}_{j}(z)|\nabla u|^{\theta p_{j}}\,dz\right)^{\frac{p_{i}(p_{j}-1)}{p_{j}}}+\varepsilon\lambda^{\theta p_{i}},

for all
i\in\{1,\cdots,m\},
hold whenever
{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}.

5.2.2.
(p,p_{i})
-phase for all
i\in\{1,\cdots,m\}

Here we state the parabolic Sobolev-Poincaré for
(p,p_{i})_{i=1}^{m}
-phases, analogous to
Lemma

3.8
and
Lemma

3.14
. The approach to proving these inequalities is the following: for a fixed
i\in\{1,\cdots,m\},
we use bounds on the
i
-th coefficient, specifically,
\frac{a_{i}(z_{0})}{2}\leq a_{i}(z)\leq 2a_{i}(z_{0}),
along with the Hölder regularity of
a_{j}(\cdot)
for
i\neq j.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
5.1
) and let the assumption (
5.5
) be in force. Then for any
\theta\in\left(\max\left\{\frac{p_{m}-1}{p},\frac{1}{p}\right\},1\right]
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1),
there exists a constant
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})
such that the following estimate

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{i,\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{i,\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta p_{i}}dz\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{i,\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\nabla u\right|^{\theta p_{i}}dz+\varepsilon\lambda^{\theta p_{i}},

for all
i\in\{1,\cdots,m\},
holds whenever
{\mathcal{P}}^{i,\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}.

5.2.3.
(p,p_{1},\cdots p_{m})
-phase

Now we state the parabolic Solbole-Poincaré for
(p,p_{1},\cdots,p_{m})
-phase. In this case, we can use the bound
\frac{a_{i}(z_{0})}{2}\leq a_{i}(z)\leq 2a_{i}(z_{0})
for every
i\in\{1,\cdots,m\}.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
5.1
) and the assumption (
5.6
) is in force. Then for any
\theta\in\left(\max\left\{\frac{p_{m}-1}{p},\frac{1}{p}\right\},1\right]
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1),
there exists a constant
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})
such that the following estimate

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\frac{u-(u)_{\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}\rho_{b}}\right|^{\theta p_{i}}dz\leq c\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})}\left|\nabla u\right|^{\theta p_{i}}dz+\varepsilon\lambda^{\theta p_{i}},

for all
i\in\{1,\cdots,m\},
holds whenever
\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{\rho_{b}}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}.

5.2.4.
Reverse Hölder inequalities

Now we briefly sketch the road map to achieve reverse Hölder inequalities for each phase, namely,
p,(p,p_{i})_{i=1}^{m},(p,p_{1},\cdots,p_{m})
-phases. We need to estimate the terms in the
Caccioppoli inequality
(
Lemma

5.1
). For the
p
-phase, we will have

(i)

\displaystyle\sup_{I^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(t_{0})}\fint_{B^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(x_{0})}\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}2\varrho}\right|^{2}\,dx\leq c\lambda^{2},

(ii)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})}H\left(z,\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}2\varrho}\right|\right)\,dz\leq c\lambda^{(1-\theta)p}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz+\varepsilon\lambda^{p}.

The above two estimates can be obtained by using
Lemma

2.1
,
Lemma

2.2
and
Lemma

5.3
. Once we have
(i)
and
(ii)
, the reverse Hölder inequality for the
p
-phase follows.

For the
(p,p_{i})_{i=1}^{m}
-phases, similarly we can have
(i)
and

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{i,\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})}H\left(z,\left|\frac{u-(u)_{{\mathcal{P}}^{i,\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}2\varrho}\right|\right)\,dz\leq c(\lambda+a_{i}(z_{0})\lambda^{p_{i}})^{(1-\theta)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{i,\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz+\varepsilon(\lambda^{p}+a_{i}(z_{0})\lambda^{p_{i}})

for each
i\in\{1,\cdots,m\},

which is again an application of
Lemma

2.1
and
Lemma

5.4
. These estimates give the reverse Hölder inequality for
(p,p_{i})_{i=1}^{m}
-phase for each
i\in\{1,\cdots,m\}.

For
(p,p_{1}\cdots,p_{m})
-phase, we will have
(i)
and

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})}H\left(z,\left|\frac{u-(u)_{\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})}}{\lambda^{-1+\mu}2\varrho}\right|\right)\,dz\leq c\Lambda^{(1-\theta)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.4445pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-12.61888pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-10.59584pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.84584pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}^{i,\lambda}_{2\varrho}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz+\varepsilon\Lambda

where
\Lambda:=\lambda^{p}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\lambda^{p_{i}}.
Applying the above estimate and
(i)
, we can deduce reverse Hölder inequality for
(p,p_{1},\cdots,p_{m})
-phase.

5.3.
Covering lemma

In this section, we briefly describe the completion of the higher integrability result for (
5.1
). The first step is to show the existence of intrinsic cylinders on the superlevel set

\displaystyle E_{\Lambda,\varrho}:=\left\{z\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\uprho}(z_{0})\,\,\Big|\,\,H\left(z,|\nabla u|\right)>\Lambda\right\},

where
H(z,\cdot)
is defined in (
5.2
) and
\Lambda=\lambda^{p}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\lambda^{p_{i}}.
Analogous to (
4.10
)-(
4.21
) in
Section

4.1
, we may have

\displaystyle K\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}\geq a_{i}(\tilde{z})\lambda^{p_{i}}_{\tilde{z}},

\displaystyle K\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}<a_{i}(\tilde{z})\lambda^{p_{i}}_{\tilde{z}},

\displaystyle a_{i}(\tilde{z})\geq 2[a_{i}]_{\alpha_{i}}\max\left\{\left(\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{\tilde{z}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}}\right)^{\alpha_{i}},\left(\frac{\lambda^{\mu}_{\tilde{z}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}}}{\sqrt{\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}+a_{i}(\tilde{z})\lambda^{p_{i}}_{\tilde{z}}}}\right)^{\alpha_{i}}\right\},

\displaystyle a_{i}(\tilde{z})<2[a_{i}]_{\alpha_{i}}\max\left\{\left(\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{\tilde{z}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}}\right)^{\alpha_{i}},\left(\frac{\lambda^{\mu}_{\tilde{z}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}}}{\sqrt{\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}+a_{i}(\tilde{z})\lambda^{p_{i}}_{\tilde{z}}}}\right)^{\alpha_{i}}\right\},

\displaystyle a_{i}(\tilde{z})\geq 2[a_{i}]_{\alpha_{i}}\max\left\{\left(\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{\tilde{z}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}}\right)^{\alpha_{i}},\left(\frac{\lambda^{\mu}_{\tilde{z}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}}}{\sqrt{\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}(\tilde{z})\lambda^{p_{i}}_{\tilde{z}}}}\right)^{\alpha_{i}}\right\},

\displaystyle a_{i}(\tilde{z})<2[a_{i}]_{\alpha_{i}}\max\left\{\left(\lambda^{-1+\mu}_{\tilde{z}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}}\right)^{\alpha_{i}},\left(\frac{\lambda^{\mu}_{\tilde{z}}\uprho_{\tilde{z}}}{\sqrt{\lambda^{p}_{\tilde{z}}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}(\tilde{z})\lambda^{p_{i}}_{\tilde{z}}}}\right)^{\alpha_{i}}\right\},

for
i=1,2,\cdots,m.
Now following the similar analysis and considering several cases carefully as in
Section

4.1
, one can complete the proof of existence of cylinders on the set
E_{\Lambda,\uprho}.

The next step involves carrying out a Vitali-type covering argument, considering
(m+2)^{2}
distinct cylinders as described in
Section

4.2
.

To complete the proof of gradient integrability, one needs to prove a result analogous to
Lemma

4.1
on each of the
(m+2)
cylinders. The rest of the proof follows from the proof of
Theorem

1.1
. The final result of the gradient higher integrability for (
5.1
) can be stated as:

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
u
be a weak solution of (
5.1
) and
\mu
be the constant defined in
Section

1.2.1

(iv)
. Then there exist positive constants
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(\textnormal{{data}})\in(0,1)
and
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})\geq 1
such that the following holds:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{r}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)^{1+\varepsilon}\,dz\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-14.75554pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-13.13002pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-9.08461pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rotatebox[origin={c}]{18.0}{$-\mkern-9.5mu-$}$}}\kern-8.33461pt}}\!\iint_{{\mathcal{P}}_{2r}(z_{0})}H(z,|\nabla u|)\,dz+1\right)^{1+\frac{\varepsilon p_{m}}{(n+2)\mu-n}},

for every
{\mathcal{P}}_{2r}(z_{0})\subset\Omega\times(0,T)
and any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
where
{\mathcal{P}}_{(\cdot)}(z_{0})
is defined in

(vi)
,
